#5087. Google Takes Aim at Nvidia With New AI Chips Built for Privacy and Massive Memory

The TPU 8tx and TPU 8i Max join Google’s eighth-generation Tensor Processing Unit lineup, giving businesses four configurations for training and running AI models. Google says it serves nearly 100 TPU customers, from AI labs to universities.

Unlike Nvidia’s widely used graphics processing units, TPUs are purpose-built AI accelerators. Google developed them for its own services but increasingly sells access to outside companies, including on-premises customers.

“With computing moving from human to agent scale, businesses are asking for accelerators that can reason across AI models’ massive context windows,” wrote Amin Vahdat, Google’s chief technologist and senior vice president of AI and infrastructure, in Google's announcement.

TPU 8tx targets a problem that many serious businesses have when it comes to adopting AI: protecting sensitive information while a model is using it.

Data is encrypted in storage and during transmission, but processing typically requires decrypting it in memory. Google says the configuration uses hardware-level confidential computing to isolate customer data and proprietary model parameters even during operation.

The system combines TPU 8t silicon with fifth-generation AMD EPYC processors and Google’s low-latency Boardfly network. Google claims it can cut latency by up to half versus TPU 8t, with almost no performance penalty for the added protection.

Companies could fine-tune and run models on the same system instead of transferring sensitive information between clusters. However, confidential computing cannot prevent a poorly designed AI agent from mishandling data it is allowed to access.

TPU 8i Max addresses another limitation: memory. Autonomous agents analyzing data, codebases, documents or hours of video, etc; need fast access to far more information than conventional AI tasks.

Google combines two TPU 8i chips into one package, doubling computing power, on-chip SRAM and high-bandwidth memory throughput versus TPU 8i. The design packs twice as much silicon into the same rack footprint.

“Our fourth system, TPU 8i Max, is built for a different problem: the memory wall,” Vahdat wrote.

Google is open-sourcing TPU Sync, software that moves AI working memory between chips, servers and storage. It claims the system can reduce certain data-transfer delays and response startup times by more than 90%.

Google's announcement comes as Nvidia faces mounting pressure on Wall Street. Shares plunged 9.3% on Thursday and fell another 5% in Monday's trading after CEO Jensen Huang admitted to an extramarital affair with a woman suspected by U.S. intelligence of spying for China. Nvidia's board has launched an independent investigation, although the company says it has no evidence that corporate secrets were compromised.

The scandal adds to concerns about Nvidia's growth as competitors target its AI chip dominance. OpenAI is reportedly planning to purchase Google's TPUs to reduce its reliance on Nvidia. This is still unconfirmed, but Google's expanding chip lineup could give AI developers more bargaining power, threatening Nvidia's market share and future growth expectations.

Google is competing on specialized hardware and software, not just chip speed. Its open-source TorchTPU project also lets developers run PyTorch and Hugging Face models on TPUs without rewriting their applications.

Still, Google’s performance figures compare its new systems with earlier Google hardware, not Nvidia’s latest products. The company did not disclose pricing or availability dates for the two new configurations.

#5084. Hyperscale Data (GPUS) Sells Its Last Bitcoin for AI Data Center, Months After Chasing $100 Million Treasury

Hyperscale Data sold its remaining 211.56 bitcoin for about $16 million by Nov. 8, bringing sales since late July to about 1,113 BTC as it races to energize 10 MW of AI capacity in Michigan in December.

Hyperscale Data has sold the last of its bitcoin, the NYSE American-listed company said Tuesday, unloading about 211.56 BTC for roughly $16 million to pay down debt and fund an AI data center in Michigan.

The sales were completed by Nov. 8 at about $76,000 per coin. Since it first sold part of its reserve in late July, Hyperscale has sold approximately 1,113 bitcoin for about $74.4 million, the company said.

That works out to an average of roughly $66,800 per coin. At today’s price near $74,200, the same 1,113 BTC would be worth about $82.6 million.

The company, which trades under the ticker GPUS, said the money will go toward its Michigan site, where it aims to energize the first 10 megawatts of critical IT capacity in December.

“We believe bringing that capacity online is our quickest path to profitability,” CEO Will Horne said in the release. “Selling our Bitcoin reserves reduces our exposure to Bitcoin price swings and gives us cash to pay down debt and fund the work in Michigan. We believe that’s the best use of this capital for all our stockholders.”

The exit is a sharp reversal from Hyperscale’s plans this summer. In late July, the company reported holding more than 1,106 bitcoin and said it wanted to build a $100 million digital asset treasury and reach full parity between its bitcoin holdings and its market capitalization.

“Every Bitcoin we acquire further strengthens Hyperscale Data’s balance sheet and expands our financial flexibility,” Executive Chairman Milton “Todd” Ault III said at the time. “We intend to continue building our Bitcoin position over time.”

A week later, Hyperscale disclosed its first sale, about 150.5 BTC, leaving it with roughly 958.5 BTC as of Aug. 2, even as its Ault Capital Group subsidiary bought about 15 BTC on the open market. The holdings were split between that unit and Sentinum. Ault said then that the company planned to keep a long-term position while tapping it for short-term flexibility, adding, “We are confident in our ability to use Bitcoin as pristine collateral.”

Tuesday’s release closes that chapter. Hyperscale said it still mines bitcoin at its Montana site and expects any bitcoin it holds on its balance sheet going forward to come “primarily” from its own mining operations.

Hyperscale is the latest public company to give up a bitcoin treasury in favor of data center spending. Keel sold its entire bitcoin treasury for $192 million in the third quarter, and Riot and Hut 8 sold thousands of bitcoin to fund AI buildouts and buybacks.

Larger miners are moving the same way. MARA said Monday it sold 13,597 BTC for about $1.1 billion after Sept. 30 and plans to keep selling to fund AI infrastructure, convertible note puts and share repurchases.

Hyperscale’s structure is more complicated than a pure-play miner’s. Its Sentinum subsidiary runs the data center business, while Ault Capital Group, a hybrid private equity firm and operating company, holds a mix of lending, defense, industrial and hotel businesses. Hyperscale expects to divest Ault Capital Group in 2027 and become a data center owner and operator.

The company said it expects to file its third-quarter 10-Q on or before Nov. 23. Because of a newly proposed exchange offer for its Class A common stock, it is also moving its virtual Investor Day from Nov. 12 to Nov. 23, when management plans to update investors on debt reduction and the December energization target.

Hyperscale did not say how much debt the bitcoin proceeds have retired so far or how much remains outstanding.

#5083. 9 Best Winter Boots for Women, Tested and Reviewed by Fashion Editors

After years of testing, our top pick is from Sorel.

Women's Health may earn commission from the links on this page, but we only feature products we believe in. Why Trust Us?

As a style writer, I live in comfortable sneakers and heels for most of the year. But come winter, my favorite shoes can’t handle New York City’s slick, slushy streets. That’s when I swap in a tried-and-true waterproof winter boot. For the past two years, WH editors have been testing tons of cold-weather shoes and have found the best options that keep our feet warm without skimping on style.

During evaluations, we put a range of comfortable boots to the test, including chic leather options for warmer climates, warm hiking boots, and insulated styles for the chilliest locales. We wore them during long, snowy days on Finland’s slopes and wet morning commutes to our New York City office. The result? A curated guide to the absolute best options for different preferences and weather conditions.

What’s New: - At the close of last winter, we added The North Face Thermoball Lace Up Waterproof Boots to our guide. It pairs well with jeans, leggings, or even a cool maxi skirt or dress. “The fit is secure, so nothing is getting through these babies,” says Saladino, who wears this for snowy weekdays in New York City and ski weekends. “They’re perfect for après-skiing, shoveling the driveway, or anytime you need complete and total protection from the elements,” she says.

Her only complaint? They are a little on the heavier side, weighing in at two pounds and one ounce.

This cozy boot is the perfect pull-on style for casual snowy days and quick outings, Saladino says. Case in point: it's ideal when you need to take the dog out or you’re jetting out the door for school drop-off. There’s also extra room inside the boot, which Saladino loves when she needs to slip it on and go.

Plus, it has a clean, simple design and cozy-chic look that you’ll actually want to wear all winter long. We love the contrast between the suede upper and the lighter-colored outsole, and think this pair looks adorable styled with a pair of wool socks and leggings.

Our only word of warning is that because the shaft height on this boot is on the shorter side, it may not be ideal for extra snowy days. If you need a boot to handle more intense winter weather, we recommend opting for The North Face Shellista IV Mid WP.

On an October trip to Iceland, I opted for this waterproof pair, since fall there is super unpredictable and rainy. While I opted for hiking boots most of the time, the Columbia Snowtrot Boot proved its waterproof capabilities on a particularly wet day of the trip, not letting a drop of water inside the shoe.

Considering how warm it kept my feet, I know I’ll be reaching for these all winter in New York. I love the puffy silhouette with its quilted upper and the monochrome finish in the all black colorway (which looks way more streamlined than similar pairs I’ve seen with white or gum soles). It’s lightweight, easy to pack, and has a sleek look that styles well with leggings and a puffer jacket or sweater. I especially like that they don't have the bulky feel I’ve come to expect from a winter boot.

Where this boot really stands out is its weather protection. It’s made with multiple layers of breathable synthetic materials and reinforced seams that help keep rain and moisture out. It also has 200 grams of insulation and a soft fleece interior to keep feet warm and cozy.

I also appreciate the side zipper, which makes them easy to slip on and off (especially handy when putting on your boots while bending over in bulky pants and jackets). The rubber outsole also helps provide tons of traction on both wet and dry surfaces, with a grippy tread pattern that comes in handy while navigating rainy sidewalks, muddy paths, or icy ground.

While it costs a fraction of the price of other winter boots, Dream Pairs makes a solid winter shoe that keeps cold and wet weather out. In addition to a water-resistant faux leather, the shoe has a plush faux-fur interior that’s super insulating. It also has a 1.37-inch rubber anti-slip sole for better traction in slippery conditions. The sole has 2mm of plush memory foam for added comfort and shock absorption. We also appreciate the pull tab on the back of the boot, which makes them easier to slip on and off.

Like our top overall pick, we like that this winter boot comes in a variety of colors: beige, nude, light gray, chestnut, and black. The cozy faux-fur lining, monochromatic laces, side panel stitching, and sturdy sole make it super stylish and practical. It easily slips under snow and ski pants, and looks just as cute styled with a pair of leggings and fuzzy socks.

On the downside, our testers said they noticed signs of wear and tear sooner than other pairs, particularly ripping of the faux-fur lining.

When I started crafting my packing list for a recent trip to Northern Finland, a cute but snow-proof winter boot was at the top of my list. After lots of research, I landed on the Columbia Minx Shorty IV Boot—a perfect blend of style, traction, water resistance, and warmth.

The moment I slipped this boot on, I loved the cozy, faux-fur insulation that kept my feet warm even on frigid mornings and evenings. And the waterproof nylon outer meant I didn’t have to worry about slushy streets or fresh snow seeping through. The traction is also super impressive, and I felt comfortable walking over icy sidewalks, a frozen lake, and snowy trails.

The shaft height hits about two inches above the ankle, offering just the right amount of coverage for typical city snow or moderate winter adventures. While deeper snow might call for a taller boot, this pair provides the ideal balance between protection and mobility.

The cherry on top? It’s genuinely so stylish. The sleek design, color selection, and faux-fur collar mean you don’t have to sacrifice fashion for function. I’ve paired them with everything from fleece-lined leggings and Fair Isle sweaters, snow pants and a winter coat, and even jeans—and they always look great. It was my go-to boot for the whole trip, and I know I’ll get just as much use out of it this winter in New York.

If your feet are perpetually cold once winter hits, meet your new favorite snow boots. The boot features an extremely thick layer of insulation in the form of a cushioned foam lining that molds to your feet over time. Wearing this boot genuinely feels like stepping into a giant marshmallow. I especially like that the platforms lift your feet off the cold, wet ground, which makes a huge difference in keeping them toasty. Plus, the shearling details add a cozy touch.

I also love Moon Boot’s iconic, space-age silhouette with its oversized shape. The Italian brand has built a reputation for seriously chic après-ski style, but don't let the fashion-forward appearance fool you. The rubber tread outsole provides plenty of grip on snowy surfaces, while the surprisingly lightweight feel makes them comfortable for everyday winter outings, whether you're heading to the lodge or walking around town after a fresh snowfall.

After testing this boot in Iceland, I can say that having a genuinely warm, insulated pair of boots makes a big difference when you're outside in the cold for extended periods. This pair is hands-down my favorite option for especially frigid locations when warmth is your biggest priority, particularly if you're looking for something that doesn't sacrifice style.

Dorenna Newton, executive producer for Men’s Health and Women’s Health, recently wore this boot in St. Moritz while covering Team USA’s bobsled training ahead of the Olympics. She loved that they looked “super chic” for après adventures in the village—earning style points along with practicality.

It’s also well-equipped for slushy snow, thanks to a waterproof leather upper to keep moisture out. The brand doesn’t have half sizes, but we found it wise to size down if you’re between sizes. “I am a size 9, and it fits great even with a thick sock,” says Saladino. Overall, we were impressed by both style and function—particularly the traction, which makes it a bit more equipped for heavy winter conditions than other ankle-length styles.

The Ugg Classic Mini Platform is a well-loved classic, and freelance stylist Alexandra Juric says they're constantly in rotation during the fall and winter. It’s stylish, comfortable, and warm. It also has a heavily cushioned EVA foam midsole that feels super plush under the foot. The suede outer adds to the cozy look and feel, though it’s less water-resistant than other leathers. We recommend snagging the shoe protector spray if you plan to wear them in wet weather. Associate health and fitness editor Addison Aloian says this boot is the perfect addition to her winter wardrobe. "I've worn them on wet, rainy, snowy streets and they've stayed relatively clean, even in a lighter color," she says. The downside? They don’t come in half sizes and tend to run big, so keep that in mind when shopping.

The type of boot you want for everyday city commuting is different from what you'd need for winter hiking or snowshoeing in seriously cold weather, says Julie Hardy, senior buyer at Zappos.

While a boot’s temperature ratings provide a useful starting point for what to look for when shopping, there's no one-size-fits-all rule, says Hardy. For most outdoor activities like snowshoeing, hiking, or backpacking, our experts suggest looking for a baseline of about 200 grams of insulation. Liners can also boost the product's warmth. Seam-sealed and waterproof features are also important if you spend a good amount of time in the snow or rain. Look for terms like "Thinsulate" and "GORE-TEX," both of which are trademarked materials that provide superior insulation.

Keep in mind that taller boots also help keep more of your leg warm even without the presence of snow. Generally, mid-height boots (about 6.5 inches) strike the perfect balance of coverage and comfort; however, tall heights (12 inches or more) are better suited for deep snow. Ultimately, shaft height is about personal preference, but make sure your boots go above the hem of your pants to keep snow and cold air out.

#5081. Global banks commit $270M to stablecoin venture targeting 2027 launch

Project Faro, backed by Goldman Sachs, Citigroup and BBVA, has secured $270 million in funding to launch a US dollar stablecoin in the first half of 2027.

A consortium of 21 financial institutions has committed about $270 million in funding to a stablecoin initiative known as Project Faro. The project, backed by big banks, aims to act as a stablecoin issuer and launch a US dollar stablecoin in the first half of 2027.

According to Bloomberg, the funding is intended to cover five years of operations for the new company, which plans to issue stablecoins pegged to the currencies of all G7 countries, with a euro-backed coin expected to follow the dollar launch.

The group first announced the initiative in September. While confirmed details are thin, people familiar with the matter said the company is expected to be incorporated in the US, Bloomberg reported.

The group includes banks such as Citigroup, Goldman Sachs, Banco Santander, Deutsche Bank and BBVA. Similar initiatives are also underway, including Stripe-led Open USD, which counts Visa among its founding partners, and Qivalis, a European banking consortium seeking to challenge the dominance of US dollar-pegged stablecoins.

BBVA is one of several Faro members also involved in competing stablecoin initiatives, including Open USD and Qivalis. Dutch lender Rabobank is also a Faro member and joined Qivalis in May, when the euro consortium added 25 banks to reach 37 members. BNP Paribas was among the 10 banks that first explored what became Faro in October 2025 and later joined Qivalis.

Qivalis has since made progress on the regulatory front, receiving authorization from the Dutch central bank as an electronic money institution in October 2026. The consortium is preparing to launch a fully regulated, euro-denominated stablecoin, with plans to make it available for payments, cross-border settlements and tokenized asset transactions.

The overlap highlights how traditional financial institutions are positioning themselves across multiple initiatives rather than committing exclusively to a single stablecoin issuance project.

By participating in competing projects, banks can explore different approaches to issuing and distributing digital currencies while retaining a stake in the infrastructure that could underpin future payment and settlement systems.

Related: BofA, Citi, Goldman Sachs among 21 institutions planning stablecoin launch

Despite the growing institutional interest, Faro is entering a market dominated by two crypto-native issuers.

Tether’s USDT and Circle’s USDC account for approximately 85% of the roughly $290 billion stablecoin market as of Nov. 10, according to DefiLlama. USDT had a market capitalization of approximately $176.3 billion, while USDC accounted for around $70.4 billion.

Distribution could, however, offer the bank-backed venture a competitive advantage. Faro’s participating institutions already have established banking customers, payment networks and relationships with businesses and financial institutions that could help distribute a new stablecoin.

Rather than building an entirely new customer base, the consortium could leverage those existing relationships to introduce its stablecoin to new audiences, including retail users. Open USD, developed by the Open Standard consortium, is pursuing a similar approach by bringing together payment providers, banks, financial technology companies and merchants around a shared stablecoin infrastructure.

The challenge for both will be translating their existing financial infrastructure and customer relationships into a competitive onchain payment network.

#5080. AI Data Centers Are Buying Used Jet Engines as New Power Turbines Take Six Years

AI Data Centers Are Buying Used Jet Engines as New Power Turbines Take Six Years

AI data-center developers are hunting for used gas turbines and retired jet engines because ordering brand-new power equipment could leave their servers waiting for electricity until the next decade.

Some companies are buying refurbished machines, while others are converting aircraft-engine technology into generators that can run alongside their computing facilities.

New gas turbines now take an average of six years to deliver in the United States, according to energy consultancy Wood Mackenzie. Delivery discussions are already focused on 2030 and 2031, a timeline that clashes with an AI industry trying to bring enormous computing campuses online much sooner.

Global turbine orders have topped 100 gigawatts, compared with manufacturers' estimated annual capacity of just 60 to 70 gigawatts, Reuters reported. Siemens Energy, GE Vernova, and Mitsubishi Heavy Industries have all said orders are outpacing what they can produce.

Gas turbines can generate large amounts of electricity by burning natural gas to spin equipment connected to a generator. AI data centers need that power around the clock to run densely packed chips and cooling systems, and connecting new facilities to public electricity grids can itself take years.

The International Energy Agency expects global data-center electricity consumption to nearly double from 485 terawatt-hours in 2025 to 950 in 2030. Consumption at AI-focused facilities is projected to triple over the same period.

One way around the backlog is an aeroderivative turbine, a power generator built using technology adapted from aircraft jet engines.

Other companies are going further by seeking older machines and retired aircraft engines that can be rebuilt for stationary power production. For example, AI infrastructure firm Crusoe ordered 13 units from Missouri-based ProEnergy in April, each rated at 50 megawatts, for a combined 650 megawatts. The jet-engine-derived equipment is intended for future U.S. data centers, but the contract doesn’t mean that Crusoe bought engines removed from retired aircraft.

Second-hand machines can also carry a substantial discount. Used turbines in good condition sell for about $1.5 million to $1.7 million per megawatt, compared with roughly $2 million to $2.5 million for new units, according to equipment brokers and energy analysts interviewed by Reuters.

One broker described a sale of two smaller turbines totaling 29 megawatts for $12.5 million. Equivalent new equipment would have cost about $35 million and taken five years to arrive, he said.

“That secondary market is very, very hot right now,” EthosEnergy CEO Ana Amicarella told Reuters, adding that asking prices are climbing as more buyers compete for the same machinery.

Building a power plant beside a data center also brings fuel needs, air emissions, and permits into the equation. The Environmental Protection Agency says stationary turbines and engines used by data centers are subject to federal air-pollution standards.

Last week, OpenAI promised to cover electricity and grid costs at future campuses it owns and operates after voters backed tighter restrictions on data-center construction.

Power-market research firm Cleanview identified about 90 gigawatts of proposed on-site generation at U.S. data centers in its mid-2026 analysis. Only about 2 gigawatts was operating at the time

Developers are looking beyond turbines, too. Finnish manufacturer Wärtsilä told Reuters it had booked around 3 gigawatts of orders from U.S. data centers since spring 2025 for generators powered by gas engines rather than turbines.

#5079. MARA Sells 13,597 Bitcoin for $1.1 Billion, Says More Sales Are Coming

MARA sold about 40% of its bitcoin stack after Sept. 30, used $750 million to repay all its BTC-backed term loans and said it will keep selling to fund AI infrastructure, convertible note puts and buybacks.

MARA Holdings sold 13,597 bitcoin for about $1.1 billion after the third quarter ended and used $750 million of the cash to pay off all of its bitcoin-backed term loans, the miner said Monday in its Q3 shareholder letter.

The coins went for roughly $81,039 each. MARA held 34,012 BTC on Sept. 30, so the sale cut about 40% of the stack in a matter of weeks.

Repaying the loans returned 17,477 bitcoin that had been pledged as collateral, leaving less than 5% of MARA’s holdings loaned or pledged, the company said. Subtracting the sale from the quarter-end figure implies about 20,400 BTC left, before counting coins mined since.

The letter makes clear the selling is not over. “We intend to continue using bitcoin to build cash for purposes such as AI infrastructure opportunities, to satisfy the puts on our convertible notes that become exercisable next year, and potential share repurchases,” MARA wrote.

The board also authorized a buyback of up to $500 million of common stock after quarter-end.

MARA spent years building one of the largest bitcoin stacks among public companies. The shift away from holding came in stages. The company said it began permitting sales of mined bitcoin in 2025 and in 2026 expanded the policy to cover bitcoin already on its balance sheet.

It sold about 26,984 bitcoin for $1.9 billion in the first nine months of the year, including 3,891 BTC in the third quarter at an average of $75,300. It mined 2,247 BTC in the quarter and bought none.

Year to date, MARA has cut total debt by $1.4 billion, or 37%. It ended September with $946.5 million in cash and about $3.8 billion in cash and bitcoin combined, plus 1,408 BTC lent out that earned about $2.5 million in interest during the quarter.

MARA’s post-quarter sale fetched about $6,800 more per coin than bitcoin’s price near $74,200 on Tuesday morning.

The quarter’s headline profit came from the coins, not the mines. Revenue fell 35% to $164.2 million from $252.4 million a year earlier, even as energized hashrate rose 18% to 71.2 EH/s.

Net income rose to $632.1 million, or $1.28 a diluted share, from $123.1 million, helped by an $885.3 million gain on the fair value of digital assets.

Mining itself got slightly more expensive. Purchased energy cost per bitcoin at MARA’s owned sites was $39,674, up from $39,235 a year earlier, while blocks won rose 2% to 644.

“Bitcoin built the foundation. Power created the opportunity,” the letter opens, describing a strategy to “maximize the value of every megawatt” MARA controls.

MARA said it is targeting two data center leases before year-end but warned that “market conditions are fluid and that timing could slip into next year.” It received FERC approval for its Long Ridge natural gas plant purchase, currently authorized to sell 485 MW, and expects to close in the coming weeks. It also secured rights to develop a Matagorda County, Texas site with up to 2 GW of power capacity, and its Exaion unit signed a four-year, $79 million GPU-as-a-service contract with its first U.S. customer.

The move puts MARA in step with peers pulling away from bitcoin. Riot and Hut 8 sold thousands of bitcoin to fund AI projects and buybacks, Keel sold its entire treasury, and Core Scientific plans to exit mining by year-end.

MARA still describes bitcoin mining as its operating foundation, one that keeps sites earning revenue while higher-value infrastructure is built out. With roughly 13,600 fewer coins on the books and a stated plan to keep selling, the miner that once carried one of the largest corporate stacks is now using that stack to pay for its next business.

#5076. CFTC warns Kalshi of imminent charges over notification of contracts: Politico

The prediction markets operator says the issue involves procedural errors affecting a handful of contracts, many of which weren’t live, according to Politico.

Prediction markets operator Kalshi is being warned of imminent charges from the US Commodity Futures Trading Commission over whether the operator properly notified the agency about new products, Politico reported Tuesday.

The Commodity Futures Trading Commission is investigating notifications involving dozens of products, the report said, citing a person with direct knowledge of the probe. The agency’s private notification to Kalshi suggests that charges could follow.

The investigation has been underway for months, the person said.

Speaking to Politico, Kalshi spokesperson Elisabeth Diana described the matter as a procedural error involving submissions for a handful of contracts, many of which never went live.

“We are already addressing the issue,” she added.

The potential enforcement action comes as the CFTC fights to establish its exclusive authority over prediction markets, including by challenging state efforts to regulate Kalshi.

Separately, Kalshi is backing the regulator in a separate legal battle over its authority. On Monday, the company joined Coinbase and four other firms in seeking permission to file a brief supporting the CFTC’s treatment of perpetual futures tied to individual US stocks.

#5074. Anthropic's Claude Fable 5.2 and Mythos 5.0 Are Impressive, But Be Prepared to Sell a Kidney to Afford Them

Anthropic's Claude Fable 5.2 and Mythos 5.0 Are Impressive, But Be Prepared to Sell a Kidney to Afford Them

Anthropic released Claude Fable 5.2 and Mythos 5.2 on Monday, promising AI models that can turn a handful of instructions into working software, design physical objects, and tackle research problems that previously required teams of specialists.

You can now ask a chatbot to build the app you've always wanted. You may also need to sell a kidney to pay for it.

That's not because every prompt costs thousands of dollars. It's because Fable can keep working for days, writing code, testing it, and fixing its own mistakes while the meter keeps running.

Some users have already tested its capabilities, and they’ve been impressed—by the power, and the price.

AI YouTuber Matthew Berman put the model through a series of ambitious tests and described Fable 5.2 as the “best AI model” he's ever used. His projects included playable video games and a browser-based version of Microsoft Excel, complete with formulas and pivot tables.

One single level of Gears of War: E-Day-inspired level took 43 hours and 48 minutes of build time and processed 8.63 billion tokens—small chunks of information an AI reads and produces. The running API cost estimate, based on pay-as-you-go pricing for developers, had reached $14,710.

I've burned over 100 billion tokens early testing Fable 5.2. It is BY FAR the best model I've ever used.

Here's my full review (including Gears of War clone):

> Fable 5.2 is the most autonomous model to date. Given a task with little direction, it makes… pic.twitter.com/0xTzqTY040

— Matthew Berman (@MatthewBerman) November 9, 2026

The Excel clone ran up an estimated $11,700 after discounts for cached inputs, or information the system can reuse instead of processing from scratch. Berman also showed a Fall Guys-style game that consumed more than 9 billion tokens over 71 hours, equivalent to roughly $13,000.

Those are estimated API costs, and the projects weren't flawless: Berman reported awkward animations, bugs, and features that still needed testing.

If you’re poor, well… Fable also knows how to work for cheap. For less than $40 (two months of Claude Pro) it built a cute orange cylinder.

Another experiment simulated water-balloon physics for $91. It’s worth noting this isn’t a model intended for routine tasks. This is for serious work and people with deep pockets.

In its launch announcement, Anthropic said the new models generate output up to 30% faster than their predecessors. The company also says Fable helped solve two open theoretical-physics problems involving black holes and design three functioning electronic devices, claims that will need independent scrutiny.

Anthropic's internal FrontierCode 2.0 evaluation, which measures the percentage of proposed coding changes that would be accepted into real open-source projects, put Fable 5.2 at 49.1%, compared with 32.4% for Fable 5.1. That helps explain why users are pushing it beyond simple chat responses.

Fable and Mythos share the same underlying model, but Fable has stricter safeguards around dangerous biological and cybersecurity requests. Mythos is reserved for verified organizations working in those fields.

The standard API price is $8 per million input tokens and $40 per million output tokens, down from the previous generation's $10 and $50. Optional fast mode can generate output up to 2.5 times faster, but doubles the rates to $16 and $80.

For contrast, Kimi 3.1 Pro, a model considered by many as the best Chinese AI model costs $2 per million input tokens and $10 per million output tokens. The latest Deepseek model costs $0.6 and $1.98 outside of peak hours.

Per Anthropic’s standards, Fable’s price is still twice the per-token price of Claude Opus 5.5, which Anthropic recommends for everyday work. Its flagship is meant for problems the cheaper models struggle to finish, not for asking whether your email sounds too aggressive.

Just last week, speculation swirled around a supposed Fable 5.5. The actual release is 5.2, with cheaper token rates but enough appetite to turn a hobby project into a serious expense.

Anthropic, last we checked, doesn’t accept kidneys as a method of payment.

#5073. Trump Media (DJT) Pledges 73% of Its Bitcoin to an Options Program as $1 Billion Note Deadline Nears

Trump Media's Q3 10-Q shows 11,385 of its roughly 15,649 bitcoin pledged to an options program whose counterparty can reuse the coins, with nearly all the rest backing $1 billion of notes holders can put back Nov. 30.

Trump Media & Technology Group has pledged about 73% of its bitcoin to an options program in which the counterparty can reuse the coins, the company disclosed in its third-quarter 10-Q filed Monday, three weeks before holders of its $1 billion in convertible notes can demand cash.

The Truth Social parent held approximately 15,649 BTC on Sept. 30, Chief Financial Officer Phillip Juhan said on Monday’s earnings call, along with 7 million shares of BlackRock’s IBIT. Of those coins, 11,385.20 were pledged to what the company calls its bitcoin yield management program.

The filing says that program requires Trump Media to post bitcoin as collateral “that the counterparty can rehypothecate at their sole discretion.” The pledged coins carried a fair value of $951.4 million at quarter-end. The 10-Q does not name the counterparty.

Another 4,260.73 BTC, worth $356.1 million on Sept. 30, sits as collateral for the convertible notes and cannot be withdrawn or distributed until the indenture’s conditions are met.

Together, the two buckets account for about 15,646 BTC, nearly all of the stack Juhan described.

Trump Media sells and buys covered calls and puts on bitcoin, settled in bitcoin, to “generate yield on these assets and increase our principal balance of bitcoin,” the filing says. Premiums arrive in bitcoin and are “immediately rehypothecated to our digital asset manager to reinvest.”

At quarter-end it had written calls referencing 4,838.40 BTC at strikes from $68,000 to $92,000 and puts on 1,500 BTC at $75,000 to $80,000, all expiring in October.

A risk factor spells out the trade-off. The company says its yield counterparties “may not be rated” by credit agencies, that it is limited in its ability to sell or use deployed bitcoin while the arrangements are open, and that where they are unsecured “we may be unable to recover deployed bitcoin in a counterparty insolvency.”

Juhan said Trump Media bought 4,359 bitcoin during the quarter with proceeds from a partial sale of its IBIT position, part of a shift toward “third-party institutional” management of its treasury.

The bitcoin rally in the third quarter turned Trump Media profitable on paper. Net income was $409.7 million, driven by a $378.1 million gain on digital assets as bitcoin climbed to about $84,000 on Sept. 30 from about $59,000 on June 30. Adjusted EBITDA was still a loss of $12.3 million, and the company lost $234.2 million over the first nine months.

Prices have since moved against the stack. With bitcoin near $74,200 Tuesday morning, the 15,649 coins are worth about $1.16 billion, roughly $145 million less than at quarter-end.

The bigger test is the notes. Holders of the $1 billion in 0% senior secured convertibles, due May 2028, can require Trump Media to buy them back at full principal for cash on Nov. 30. General Counsel Scott Glabe said notices have gone out and holders have until near the end of the month to respond, so the company cannot yet say how much it will owe.

Interim CEO Kevin McGurn said the plan is “a mix of cash and credit” rather than new equity. “We want to preserve as much of our Bitcoin as possible to continue our yielding strategy and allow that asset to recover,” he said.

The 10-Q says the company may enter a new credit facility to refinance some or all of the notes, but adds that “there can be no assurance” it can do so on acceptable terms. Juhan put gross financial assets at $2.3 billion, including $182 million in cash, $211 million in short-term investments and $1.7 billion in bitcoin and bitcoin-related assets.

Trump Media is not the only treasury company under pressure. Strategy, the largest corporate holder, slipped below its average cost on Monday. Trump Media also faces more political scrutiny after Democrats won Congress, with party leaders vowing to target Trump family crypto ventures and incoming Senate Banking Chair Elizabeth Warren preparing her own oversight push.

Shareholders vote Dec. 14 on the company’s planned merger with fusion energy firm TAE Technologies, which management expects to close by year-end.

#5070. Miami-Dade County Now Accepts Bitcoin for Property Taxes

Miami-Dade County Tax Collector Dariel Fernandez said Monday his office has begun accepting bitcoin for property tax payments, both online and in person, while converting every transaction into dollars before the money reaches the county.

Fernandez announced the change in a statement on the Tax Collector’s website, calling it a way to give property owners another option without putting public funds into the asset. Payers can select a cryptocurrency option during online checkout or request it at the counter, then connect a supported exchange account or digital wallet and authorize the payment.

A third-party system converts the bitcoin into U.S. dollars at the time of payment, the office said, so it never holds, invests in or manages any cryptocurrency. The office said it still receives the full dollar amount owed and records the transactions in its standard payment reports, without county workers maintaining digital wallets.

“Government must keep up with the times and the needs of the people we serve,” Fernandez said. “By accepting Bitcoin as a payment option, we are giving property owners another way to pay their taxes, whether online or in person, while continuing to offer the traditional payment methods they already know and trust.”

He added that the office is “embracing new technology without compromising our responsibility to protect taxpayer dollars.” Whether someone pays with bitcoin or a conventional method, he said, “our office receives the full amount owed in U.S. dollars.”

The office did not name the payment processor or say whether bitcoin payments carry a convenience fee. Card and mobile-wallet payments already carry a nonrefundable 2.50% fee online, with a $2.50 minimum, the office’s payment-methods page shows. Traditional options, including checks, cards, cash and fee-free eChecks, remain available. The announcement covers property taxes, and the option is voluntary.

JUST IN: 🇺🇸 Miami-Dade now accepts Bitcoin for property taxes and can be paid both online and in person 👀 pic.twitter.com/lI285z4B79

The change landed in the middle of Florida’s property tax collection season. Miami-Dade opened the 2026 tax roll on Oct. 30 and is urging owners to pay by Nov. 30 to lock in a 4% early-payment discount under Florida law. The discount falls to 3% in December, 2% in January and 1% in February, with the full amount due in March. Unpaid bills generally become delinquent April 1.

Under the office’s existing rules, discounts are based on the date a payment is submitted online or in person. Fernandez has been promoting a “Save the Max” campaign around that calendar, noting that an eligible $5,000 tax bill would save $200 if paid in November. Payments can be made at the office’s 13 locations, through T-Connect mobile units or online around the clock at mdctaxcollector.gov.

The move closes a loop the county opened years ago. In 2021, Miami-Dade Commissioner Danielle Cohen Higgins put forward a resolution to study letting residents pay county taxes and fees with bitcoin, after Miami Mayor Francis Suarez pushed to let residents pay city fees and taxes in bitcoin and explored paying city workers in it. The city of Miami also launched MiamiCoin, a token tied to the Stacks protocol, as part of that campaign.

Fernandez, elected in November 2024, had earlier served on Miami-Dade’s Cryptocurrency Task Force, his official biography notes.

The office called Miami-Dade one of the first government agencies in the country to accept the payments. Colorado has taken cryptocurrency for state taxes since 2022, converting it to dollars through a third party, and Utah later opened a similar option for individual income taxes. Detroit announced in 2024 that it would start accepting cryptocurrency for city taxes and fees in mid-2025, also settling in dollars.

Florida lawmakers, meanwhile, revived a 2026 bill to create a state Strategic Cryptocurrency Reserve after a 2025 effort was withdrawn. The Senate measure, CS/SB 1038, died in an appropriations committee in March and never became law.

For now, Miami-Dade is offering a narrower door: bitcoin can be used to pay the property tax bill, and the county keeps none of it.

#5068. 'Tough Questions' Ahead: Warren Targets Prediction Markets, Trump Crypto as Banking Chair

'Tough Questions' Ahead: Warren Targets Prediction Markets, Trump Crypto as Banking Chair

Sen. Elizabeth Warren (D-MA), one of the crypto industry's most prominent critics, is preparing to use the chairmanship of the Senate Banking Committee to launch an oversight campaign aimed at Trump family crypto businesses, Big Tech and Wall Street, Politico reported on Monday.

"Big Tech CEOs, Administration officials, Trump family crypto businesses, Wall Street executives, and giant corporations should preserve their documents and prepare to answer tough questions," a Warren Banking Committee aide told the outlet.

Warren is expected to take over the committee in January after Democrats won control of the Senate in last week's midterm elections. The panel oversees banking, housing, cryptocurrency and the Federal Reserve, and its chair has broad powers to call hearings and launch investigations.

According to the aide, Warren also wants to end a "Wild West-style lack of oversight" of prediction markets that she says has allowed insiders to profit, and plans to warn about threats to financial stability, including a potential AI bubble.

She intends to keep working with Republican China hawks to restrict exports of advanced AI chips, and Nvidia CEO Jensen Huang is expected to be a top oversight target, Politico reported. The report came a day after Nvidia's board opened an independent investigation into reports that Huang had been the target of a Chinese intelligence effort, sending Nvidia shares sliding as much as 8%.

Warren is also likely to investigate the Office of the Comptroller of the Currency's August decision to grant a conditional bank charter to Trump-backed World Liberty Financial, as well as the administration's firings of financial agency staff and its efforts to shut down the Consumer Financial Protection Bureau, Reuters reported, citing a person familiar with her thinking. "You're going to see a lot of investigations," Brian Gardner, chief Washington policy strategist at Stifel, told the outlet.

In an essay for Time last week, Warren argued that Democrats won by "being populist economic fighters," and said "Big Tech CEOs, Wall Street executives, and giant corporations should prepare to answer tough questions and face meaningful consequences if they have broken the law."

Warren has been a persistent critic of the crypto industry and of the Trump family's business interests in it. She has argued that federal regulators' approval of national trust charters for crypto firms, including Coinbase and Ripple, is illegal, and has said the CFTC has been "steamrolled" by the prediction market and crypto firms it oversees.

She has also questioned Commerce Secretary Howard Lutnick over a Tether loan to a trust tied to his children, and joined Sen. Bernie Sanders (I-VT) in urging the Labor Department to drop plans that would make it easier to hold crypto in 401(k) retirement plans.

Warren was one of the leading opponents of the Clarity Act, the crypto industry's top legislative priority, which stalled in the Senate this year. Democrats are now planning to push a broader crypto market structure bill when they take control of Congress.

The White House has pushed back. A spokesperson told Reuters that Trump is willing to work with anyone on behalf of Americans, but that "sham investigations and radical left policies" would undermine any such effort.

Republicans have cast Warren as the main obstacle to crypto legislation. Sen. Tim Scott (R-SC), the current Banking chair, has argued that without market structure rules, crypto is left in "the wild, wild West."

Warren's ability to pass legislation will be limited while Trump remains in the White House. In her Time essay, she acknowledged that Trump has embraced the nickname "Mr. Veto," but argued that Democrats should send him bills anyway.

Warren is also eyeing a more senior role in Senate Democratic leadership, potentially the No. 3 position, Politico reported last month.

#5067. LNbits rushes emergency patch after reported Bitcoin wallet thefts

The project warned that installations running older versions were vulnerable and urged operators to replace potentially exposed credentials even after upgrading.

Bitcoin Lightning wallet platform LNbits has urged users to update immediately to its latest version after reporting a wave of attacks targeting LNbits instances that resulted in stolen funds.

The project published version 1.7.0 on Tuesday, describing it as a critical release addressing vulnerabilities and strengthening authentication, extension management and payment handling. LNbits warned that updating alone would not protect installations whose credentials had already been compromised.

“It’s been a very challenging few days, working relentlessly to get the v1.7 release out after our users and we suffered losses,” LNbits creator Ben Arc said on X.

LNbits provides software for running Bitcoin Lightning wallets and payment applications, including merchant checkouts, tipping services and paywalls. Developer Ben Arc said in May that about 200,000 people run the software themselves.

LNbits first warned operators on Sunday to disable its Paywall extension while it investigated a potential vulnerability. Just over an hour later, the project urged users to shut down their LNbits servers immediately, citing a wave of attacks. It also took its own hosted installations offline.

The company has not yet detailed the exploit and how many were impacted by it. In an announcement accompanying the emergency patch, LNbits said the vulnerability affects all versions before 1.7.0 and puts funds at risk of theft.

Related: BitMart founder says security team ‘severely understaffed’ after new hack

It also warned that credentials exposed before upgrading could still give attackers access afterward, urging operators to reset wallet keys, replace funding-source credentials and treat secrets stored in LNbits settings as compromised and recommended moving to a fresh server if a compromise is suspected.

Cointelegraph reached out to Ben Arc and LNbits for comment.

One user, Kevin Ravensberg, said attackers exploited his LNbits instance, withdrawing about 2.3 million satoshis, or 0.023 Bitcoin, through newly created accounts before he could shut down the server.

The company said a separate report detailing the exploit would follow.

#5064. Binance Warns iOS Flaw Could Expose Wallet Seed Phrases as Apple’s Latest iPhone Patch Stays Silent

Binance warned iPhone users Tuesday that a publicly released iOS flaw could let malicious apps read files stored by other apps, including the seed phrases and private keys that control crypto wallets.

“A vulnerability (worse_query) affecting iOS 26.7.1, 27.0.1, and 27.2 allows malicious apps to read local files from other apps, putting seed phrases, private keys, and login credentials at risk across the entire ecosystem,” the exchange wrote on X. “This is an iOS-level issue, not unique to Binance.”

Binance told users to delete any apps installed from untrusted sources, such as sideloaded or manually signed IPA files, and to update iOS and every app to the latest official versions. Anyone who suspects a breach should move funds to a new wallet created on a clean device and reset all active login sessions, the exchange said.

Neither Binance nor the researcher behind the flaw has reported any thefts tied to it.

The bug is a sandbox escape. On iOS, each app is supposed to live in its own container that other apps cannot read. A researcher who posts as forcequitOS released worse_query on GitHub on Nov. 8, calling it “a zero-day sandbox escape for iOS 26 and 27, again.” It succeeds an earlier exploit, bad_query.

The project’s README says it works on iOS 26.0 through 26.7.2 and on iOS 27.2, a wider range than the versions Binance named. It lists paths it can reach, including other apps’ data containers and shared app group folders.

The researcher describes it as “a proof-of-concept for developers” that “isn’t useful by itself,” and stresses it is not a jailbreak. To use it, a developer has to build it into an app.

That has already happened in at least one case. A day after the release, developer Christoph Kolbicz published a file manager built on worse_query that lets users browse and edit app data on iOS 27 without jailbreaking, which he said was tested on iOS 27.0.1.

Blockchain security firm SlowMist called worse_query a container sandbox escape rather than a jailbreak. Its chief information security officer, who posts as 23pds, wrote that the flaw will not steal assets on its own. The risk comes when a user installs and runs an untrusted IPA that then reads other apps’ containers, and only if a wallet stores mnemonic phrases, keystores or WebView caches in plain text on the device.

Apple shipped iOS 27.2.1 and iOS 26.7.3 on Nov. 9, a day after the proof of concept went public. Their security notes list fixes for AirPlay, Bluetooth pairing, brotli and other components, but neither names worse_query or describes a sandbox escape of this kind.

That leaves it unclear whether the latest iOS builds block the technique.

The exposure depends heavily on how each wallet app stores secrets. By SlowMist’s reading, wallets that do not write recovery phrases or keys to plain files on the device are not exposed in the same way, and hardware wallets keep private keys off the phone entirely. The attack also requires a user to install software from outside the App Store first.

The warning comes from an exchange still recovering from its own breach. Binance lost $6.34 billion in an Oct. 29 attack that began with a fake job interview. Security worries have also pushed some large holders to swap self-custodied bitcoin for IBIT shares, while hardware wallet maker Ledger said an organized crime group spent millions targeting its users.

For iPhone users holding bitcoin in a mobile wallet, Binance’s advice amounts to basic hygiene: stay on official iOS releases, skip sideloaded apps and move funds if a device may have been compromised.

#5063. Nvidia, Jensen Huang Sued by Shareholders Over Alleged Ties to Suspected Chinese Spy

Nvidia, Jensen Huang Sued by Shareholders Over Alleged Ties to Suspected Chinese Spy

Nvidia and CEO Jensen Huang have been sued by a shareholder who accuses them of defrauding investors by concealing Huang's alleged relationship with a suspected Chinese spy.

The proposed class action, filed in the U.S. District Court for the Northern District of California on Monday, accuses Nvidia and Huang of making materially false and misleading statements about the company's dealings with Megaspeed, a Singapore-based Nvidia customer, according to the complaint. It alleges that Huang "had an affair with Alice Huang, a Megaspeed executive and suspected Chinese spy," and that the defendants failed to disclose that this raised national security concerns posing a material risk to Nvidia's reputation and business prospects.

The plaintiff, Mark Kistenmacher, seeks to represent investors who bought Nvidia stock between October 9, 2025, and November 8, 2026. The suit accuses Nvidia and Huang of violating Section 10(b) of the Securities Exchange Act and Rule 10b-5, and Huang of violating Section 20(a), which can hold executives liable alongside a company. It seeks unspecified damages and a jury trial. Kistenmacher is represented by The Rosen Law Firm.

The complaint targets four statements. Two came from Nvidia spokesperson John Rizzo in October 2025, when he told The New York Times that Nvidia's employees had "found no evidence of diversion" at Megaspeed and that Huang's visits to customer events were routine. A third came from Rizzo last month, when he said Huang's interactions with Alice Huang were "not unusual or unexpected." The fourth was Huang's own description of Nvidia's dealings with Megaspeed as "normal and not unusual" in an interview with Bloomberg on October 30. Each was misleading, the suit alleges, because of Huang's relationship with Alice Huang.

Alice Huang, a former Megaspeed executive, is not related to Jensen Huang, Reuters reported. The New York Times reported on Sunday that Nvidia opened its investigation after Huang acknowledged to the company's board that he had been intimate with Alice Huang. The newspaper also reported that U.S. Secretary of State Marco Rubio oversaw the drafting of a classified memo this spring warning top administration officials about the relationship, which was circulated ahead of U.S. President Donald Trump's trip to China in May.

Decrypt has reached out to Nvidia for comment. The allegations in the complaint have not been tested in court, and it is common for shareholders to sue companies after sharp share price declines.

The lawsuit was filed five minutes after U.S. markets closed on Monday, according to Reuters. Nvidia shares closed down 4.3% at $178.67 that day, according to Yahoo Finance, reducing its market value by about $190 billion, after having slid as much as 8% earlier in the session. The stock was flat on the day at $179.70 in premarket trading on Tuesday.

Nvidia's board opened an independent investigation on Sunday into reports that Huang was the target of a foreign intelligence effort, saying it had no reason to believe that national security or company secrets had been compromised. In an email to staff the same day, Huang apologized for being unfaithful to his wife, without naming the person involved.

The New York Times reported last month that U.S. officials had concluded Alice Huang was a Chinese intelligence asset, and the Financial Times later reported that a U.S. government memo said Jensen Huang had been romantically involved with her. China's embassy in Washington has dismissed the allegations as "groundless smears."

Trump weighed in on Monday. "I asked him about that and he said he had absolutely no idea that the person in question was a spy," the president told reporters, according to The Guardian. He added that the FBI was "looking into it in terms of something else," without elaborating. A day earlier, Trump told reporters he did not regret his decision not to act on the intelligence, and that he believed Huang should stay on as CEO. "I think he's an honorable guy," he said, according to the Times.

#5060. Canaan (CAN) Swings to an $8 Million Profit on Bitcoin Gains as Rig Sales Collapse 96%

Canaan Inc. (NASDAQ: CAN) swung to $8.0 million of third-quarter net income on a $51.9 million fair-value gain in its bitcoin holdings and derivatives, the mining-rig maker said Tuesday, even as machine sales collapsed 96%.

The company filed the unaudited results Tuesday in a 6-K with the Securities and Exchange Commission. Total revenue fell to $14.5 million from $150.5 million a year earlier, including $4.8 million from product sales and $9.5 million from mining. Canaan sold 1.0 million terahashes per second of computing power and mined 133 bitcoin.

The profit did not come from the business. Canaan posted an $8.6 million gross loss and a $42.9 million operating loss. Chief financial officer Jin “James” Cheng said a recovery in bitcoin prices toward quarter-end generated the $51.9 million fair-value gain and called it “distinct from underlying operating performance.”

The $51.9 million is the sum of a $20.1 million gain on cryptocurrency and a $31.9 million gain on financial derivatives. Canaan also reported $22.4 million of non-GAAP adjusted EBITDA, a figure that still includes those gains. Strip them out and adjusted EBITDA would have been a loss of about $29.5 million.

Nangeng Zhang, Canaan’s chairman and chief executive officer, pointed to “continued weakness in bitcoin mining equipment demand and intense industry pricing competition.” Products revenue dropped from $13.6 million in the second quarter and from $118.6 million in the same period of 2025. The company blamed lower volumes and a lower average selling price.

Canaan took $8.2 million of inventory-related write-downs, down from $25.3 million in the second quarter. Mining operations posted a gross margin of about 30% before depreciation, with an all-in power cost of about $0.043 per kilowatt-hour and roughly 10 exahashes per second of installed capacity outside joint ventures.

The company held 1,875.5 bitcoin as of Sept. 30. Of that, 517.5 coins were owned outright, 1,258 were pledged for secured term loans and 100 sat in a fixed-term product. It booked $43.7 million of cryptocurrency assets and $114.2 million of cryptocurrency receivables.

The stack is down about 40 bitcoin from the 1,915.5 Canaan reported at the end of the second quarter, even after the company mined 133 in the period, implying it moved roughly 173 bitcoin off the treasury. Zhang said Canaan is “selectively monetizing cryptocurrency holdings to fund share repurchases.”

On Nov. 6, the board approved a new $100 million share repurchase authorization over 12 months and launched an initial $20 million program under that cap.

Canaan is trying to recast itself around power and compute infrastructure. Its 1-gigawatt project in Duval County, Texas, received a conditional “Studied Load – Firm” classification under ERCOT’s Batch Zero process, a grid-connection step Zhang said remains “subject to further studies and approvals.” A second 1-gigawatt project in Zapata County is in early-stage development.

To support those plans, Canaan set aside $50 million of restricted cash as collateral for a letter of credit tied to infrastructure contracts and made about $3.3 million of project prepayments. In October it bought a parcel of U.S. land for about $1.1 million. Cash ended Sept. 30 at $72.8 million, $50 million of it restricted, leaving $22.8 million unrestricted.

Canaan also plans a 1-for-25 reverse ADS split, effective on or about Dec. 4, changing each ADS from 15 Class A ordinary shares to 375. The move is meant to help the company regain compliance with Nasdaq’s minimum bid price requirement.

For the fourth quarter, Canaan expects revenue of $18 million to $22 million.

The shift toward power and compute puts Canaan in the same lane as other public bitcoin miners. MARA sold 13,597 bitcoin to fund AI infrastructure, and Hyperscale Data sold its last coins for a Michigan data center.

Whether a paper gain on 1,875 bitcoin and a pair of early-stage Texas power projects can carry Canaan until customers start buying machines again is the question the quarter leaves open.

#5059. Binance Halts MEXC Transfers as FinCEN Cuts Exchange Off From US Financial System

Binance Halts MEXC Transfers as FinCEN Cuts Exchange Off From US Financial System

Binance has stopped processing transactions involving MEXC, one of the world's largest crypto exchanges, days after the U.S. Treasury Department designated MEXC a primary money laundering concern.

"Effective immediately, users should not send funds to, receive funds from, or otherwise transact through Binance involving MEXC, MEXC Global, MEXC affiliates or related entities," Binance tweeted on Tuesday.

Binance is applying enhanced compliance measures following FinCEN’s designation of MEXC as a Primary Money Laundering Concern.

Effective immediately, users should not send funds to, receive funds from, or otherwise transact through Binance involving MEXC, MEXC Global, MEXC…

Any request to send funds from a Binance account to MEXC will be declined, according to a Binance announcement that lists 13 entities covered by the restriction, including MEXC Global Limited, MEXC Estonia OÜ and MEXC Singapore Pte Ltd. Deposits received from those entities will be temporarily withheld for compliance review, and users could face further restrictions, including being offboarded from Binance, the exchange said.

MEXC ranks ninth among spot exchanges on CoinMarketCap, which scores platforms on traffic, liquidity and trading volume, with about $812.5 million in spot volume over the past 24 hours. Binance ranks first, with about $6.4 billion.

The Treasury's Financial Crimes Enforcement Network (FinCEN) named MEXC a primary money laundering concern last Friday, in connection with Russian illicit finance. The order, issued under the Combating Russian Money Laundering Act, bars U.S. financial institutions from processing fund transfers involving the exchange.

FinCEN alleges that MEXC processed at least $16 billion in suspicious transactions between May 2021 and May 2026, including nearly $1.5 billion in direct and indirect exposure to Russian sanctions evasion, and described its anti-money laundering controls as severely deficient.

The order takes effect 30 days after its publication in the Federal Register.

Binance is not the only exchange to respond. OKX has also warned its users against transferring funds to MEXC, Finance Magnates reported.

#5055. Poland’s Parliament Votes 240-202 to Let Officials Freeze Crypto Before Court Review

Poland's Sejm passed a sanctions bill Friday that lets the interior minister freeze bitcoin and bank funds of people on a national list, and a court appeal would not pause the freeze.

Poland’s lower house of parliament voted 240-202 on Friday to let the interior minister freeze crypto assets, bank accounts and other property of people placed on a national sanctions list, before any court reviews the order.

The Sejm, Poland’s lower house, passed the Act on Restrictive Measures at its 68th sitting, with one member abstaining and 17 not voting. The Senate received the bill Monday and has 30 days under Article 121 of the Constitution to adopt, amend or reject it, after which it would go to President Karol Nawrocki.

The law, a government bill that also implements European Union sanctions rules, treats crypto assets as financial means that can be frozen, using the definition in the EU’s Markets in Crypto-Assets regulation. A freeze, as the text defines it, is meant to stop any movement, transfer, use or other change that would let the holder benefit from the assets.

The freeze applies only to people and entities the interior minister adds to a public national list, either on the minister’s own initiative or at the request of agencies that include the financial watchdog, the central bank, the financial intelligence unit and the internal security service. Grounds include supporting a country already under EU sanctions, serious human rights abuses, or initiating or financing information manipulation that poses a serious threat to Poland’s national security.

A listing on that last ground requires a showing of a real and serious threat to Poland’s interests, borders, security or elections. The bill carves out ordinary journalism, political speech and publishing, unless that activity is a knowing campaign of the kind the law describes.

The interior minister’s decision takes effect the day after it is posted in the ministry’s Public Information Bulletin and is immediately enforceable. There is no request to the minister to reconsider. A listed person may file a complaint with an administrative court, but the law says the decision remains in force until a judgment becomes final, and it sets aside the usual rule that would let a court pause a challenged order.

The measures also bar making funds or property available to listed persons and can exclude them from public contracts. In some cases, the economy minister may appoint a temporary administrator to sell frozen assets or, under a separate order, take them over for the State Treasury.

The Poland bill does not target ordinary bitcoin holders in Poland. It reaches crypto assets owned, held or under the control of people and firms on the sanctions list. Banks, exchanges and other parties face administrative fines if they fail to freeze those assets or make them available, and compliance is checked by the financial intelligence unit and the Polish Financial Supervision Authority for the firms they already oversee.

The text does not describe a way for officials to obtain the keys to coins held in self-custody. The practical enforcement path in Poland runs through exchanges, custodians and other obliged institutions that can actually stop a transfer.

The bill still has to clear the Senate and Nawrocki’s desk. The president refused to sign a separate crypto-assets market bill the Sejm passed on May 15, and lawmakers in September failed to override that veto, voting 241-198. He had also vetoed earlier versions of that market bill, arguing the rules went too far.

That earlier fight was about licensing and supervision under the EU’s crypto-assets rulebook, not about a sanctions freeze. Whether the Senate trims the pre-court freeze, and whether Nawrocki will sign a sanctions law that reaches bitcoin, is the question the bill now leaves open.

#5054. Binance Warns of Apple iOS Flaw That Could Expose iPhone Users' Private Keys

Binance Warns of Apple iOS Flaw That Could Expose iPhone Users' Private Keys

Binance has warned iPhone users about a vulnerability in Apple's iOS that it says could let malicious apps read files stored by other apps, putting crypto wallet seed phrases, private keys and login credentials at risk.

The flaw, dubbed "worse_query," affects iOS 26.7.1, 27.0.1 and 27.2, the exchange tweeted on Tuesday. "This is an iOS-level issue, not unique to Binance," it said.

A vulnerability (worse_query) affecting iOS 26.7.1, 27.0.1, and 27.2 allows malicious apps to read local files from other apps, putting seed phrases, private keys, and login credentials at risk across the entire ecosystem. This is an iOS-level issue, not unique to Binance.…

Binance urged users to delete any apps installed from untrusted sources, such as sideloaded or manually signed app files, and to update iOS and all apps to the latest official versions. Anyone who suspects a breach should move their funds to a new wallet created on a clean device and reset all active login sessions, it said.

iPhone apps normally run inside a sandbox, which stops them from accessing other apps' private files. A researcher who goes by forcequitOS published worse_query on GitHub as a sandbox escape for iOS 26.0 through 26.7.2 and iOS 27.2, describing it as a proof of concept for developers that "isn't useful by itself." Among the locations it can reach is the folder where iOS apps store their data, according to the repository, which calls it the "direct successor" to an earlier exploit, bad_query. There have been no confirmed reports of crypto being stolen using the flaw.

Apple released iOS 27.2.1 on Monday, a day after the proof of concept was published. Its security notes list fixes for nine vulnerabilities, including an authorization issue in App Intents through which "an app may be able to access sensitive user data," as well as several Bluetooth and iMessage flaws.

However, the notes do not mention worse_query or its researcher, and Apple has not publicly confirmed whether the update addresses the flaw Binance described. At least one security expert believes it does. 23pds, chief information security officer at blockchain security firm SlowMist, urged users to update to iOS 27.2.1, saying in a tweet that the update would block the worse_query sandbox escape, along with other threats.

It is not the first time attackers have targeted Apple devices. Earlier this year, Google warned of iOS malware that targeted crypto apps on unpatched devices, while in August, the Dutch national cyber agency said hackers were exploiting a flaw in Apple's macOS Screen Sharing feature to plant Monero miners on Macs.

#5052. Texas Rep. Files Bill Letting Cities Invest $10 Million in Bitcoin

A Texas lawmaker filed a bill Monday that would let each city and county in the state invest up to $10 million of public money in bitcoin or another cryptocurrency, without setting any rules for how those coins would be held.

House Bill 815, filed by Rep. Ron Reynolds, a Missouri City Democrat, on the first day of prefiling for the 90th Texas Legislature, carries the caption “Relating to the authority of a municipality or county to invest money in cryptocurrencies.” The introduced text, posted on the Texas Legislature’s site, is two sections long.

The bill would amend the Public Funds Investment Act, Chapter 2256 of the Texas Government Code, by adding a new Section 2256.0165, titled “Authorized Investments: Cryptocurrency.” Under that section, “a municipality or county may invest not more than $10 million of its money or money under its control in Bitcoin or another cryptocurrency.” The authority would take effect Sept. 1, 2027, if the bill becomes law.

The $10 million figure is a cap on each city or county, not a single statewide pool. School districts and other local governments covered by the investment act are left out.

The measure borrows its definitions of “Bitcoin” and “cryptocurrency” from Section 403.701 of the Government Code, the definitions written into law last year by Senate Bill 21, which created the Texas Strategic Bitcoin Reserve. Bitcoin is defined as a decentralized cryptocurrency created by a peer-to-peer network that operates independently of any central authority or bank. Cryptocurrency is defined more broadly as a virtual currency that uses cryptography to secure transactions recorded on a distributed ledger, such as a blockchain.

That means a city or county would not be limited to bitcoin. The bill also does not import the reserve law’s requirement that coins bought for the state fund have an average market capitalization of at least $500 billion over the most recent 24-month period, or its provisions on cold storage and qualified custodians.

The Public Funds Investment Act already requires local governments to adopt a written investment policy that emphasizes safety of principal and liquidity, and to invest with the care a prudent person would use in managing their own affairs. A city or county would still have to add cryptocurrency to that policy before buying any. The new section itself is silent on custody, on audits specific to digital assets, and on how a loss would be handled.

Reynolds filed a broader measure, House Bill 4258, on March 10, 2025, with Rep. Mihaela Plesa. That bill would have let the comptroller invest as much as $250 million of the Economic Stabilization Fund, the state’s rainy day fund, in bitcoin or another cryptocurrency, and it included the same $10 million cap for cities and counties. It was referred to the House Delivery of Government Efficiency Committee on April 1, 2025, and did not become law.

House Bill 815 drops the rainy day fund language and keeps only the local-government piece. The state’s own reserve was created separately through Senate Bill 21, which Gov. Greg Abbott signed in June 2025.

The Texas Comptroller’s office later bought $5 million of BlackRock’s iShares Bitcoin Trust on Nov. 20, 2025, as a placeholder while it worked on direct custody, The Texas Tribune reported, with the office confirming the purchase. A second purchase brought the reserve’s IBIT exposure to about $10 million. In May, Acting Comptroller Kelly Hancock named an advisory committee and issued a request for proposals for a custodian, a step toward holding bitcoin directly.

Prefiling for the 90th Texas Legislature opened Monday, and the regular session convenes Jan. 12, 2027. The bill has been filed but has not yet been referred to a committee, a step that typically waits until lawmakers convene. Even if it passes both chambers and is signed, cities and counties could not use the new authority until Sept. 1, 2027.

#5050. Morning Minute: Vitalik Recasts ETH as a Store of Value

Morning Minute: Vitalik Recasts ETH as a Store of Value

Morning Minute is a daily newsletter written by Tyler Warner. The analysis and opinions expressed are his own and do not necessarily reflect those of Decrypt. And check out our daily news show 'FOMO HOUR' covering all of the top stories and market action.

🏛️ Vitalik Recasts ETH as a Store of Value

Vitalik Buterin spent Sunday on X telling Ethereum holders to stop valuing ETH as a fee-burning token and start valuing it as a store of value. “We should not feel sad if fee burn is less than 10% of breakeven indefinitely,” he wrote. “The value of ETH comes from elsewhere.”

That elsewhere is stability, which he ties to decentralization, censorship resistance and security. It replaces the ultrasound money pitch, the idea that network usage burns supply faster than staking creates it. The burn has not kept up in years. Through the first nine months of 2026 it offset about 2% of issuance while supply grew 0.64%, mostly because Ethereum’s own rollup roadmap moved activity and fees off the mainnet.

Buterin’s argument is that this is fine, and that chasing the burn would be worse. Maximizing fee revenue means wanting congestion and expensive blockspace. He warned it would turn Ethereum into “a world-scale major corporation” at the cost of what makes it worth using. The burn, he added, was “never a true floor” and came mostly from internal crypto churn like lending and “silly NFT purchases.”

Supporters are already building out the new frame. Ethlabs co-founder Binji Pande calls ETH a future-proof store of value and argues it beats Bitcoin on quantum and AI risk, citing eleven years of trading history and an Ethereum Foundation that holds under 0.2% of supply. Julian Ma of Ethlabs makes the related case that Ethereum’s security culture positions it as a programmable store of value as AI pressures cryptography.

#5048. Across Protocol restores website after hackers hijack domain

The cross-chain bridge said a social engineering attack targeting domain registrar Namecheap allowed hackers to redirect users to a phishing site.

Cross-chain bridge Across Protocol said it has regained control of its website after a social engineering attack on Tuesday enabled attackers to gain control of its domain, replacing its website with a phishing site.

In a post on Wednesday, Across said the across.to domain is back under their control with help from Namecheap founder Richard Kirkendall, and that all systems are now working normally.

“All affected users will be made whole. We’ll share a full post-mortem in the coming days,” the team said on X.

The update follows Across’s Tuesday warning to users to avoid its app amid reports of drained wallets. The total losses and number of affected users have not yet been disclosed.

In an earlier update, Across said a social engineering attack on its domain registrar, Namecheap, gave an attacker control of its domain at approximately 18:45 UTC.

The team said it was alerted within 15 minutes and immediately worked to shut the website down. Security response group SEAL911 helped flag the domain as phishing with major wallets, while Cloudflare marked it as at risk and later removed its DNS records.

Some users who visited the domain before the malicious site was fully blocked may have been affected, the team said. Third-party integrations and API users were unaffected.

The incident affected one of the largest cross-chain bridges by trading activity. Across ranked third by 30-day transfer volume, behind Circle and LayerZero, according to DefiLlama, having processed about $5.9 billion over the period.
