#5046. LNbits Releases 1.7.0 Urgent Security Update After November 8 Attacks Drain Funds

LNbits, the open-source Lightning Network wallet and accounts platform, released version 1.7.0 on November 10 as an urgent security update and instructed all operators to upgrade before restarting their servers. The project’s official X account posted the announcement with a link to the GitHub release, calling it a “SECURITY UPDATE”.

The release follows a wave of attacks that began over the weekend. On November 8, LNbits posted on X that all users should stop their LNbits servers immediately after reporting active attacks, adding that security patches were being prepared and that it was “better to err on the side of caution.”

The project also disabled instances on its hosted service. Multiple reports documented unauthorized account creation and fund withdrawals.

Kevin Ravensberg, operator of the Telegram tipping service BitTipBot, publicly stated that attackers created new accounts on his instance and withdrew 2,285,545 satoshis (approximately 0.0229 BTC) between roughly 09:22 and 14:00 UTC on November 8.

According to the official upgrade guidance published alongside the release, versions prior to 1.7.0 are affected and “funds running on prior versions of LNbits are at risk of being stolen.” The project stated that 1.7.0 includes security fixes in response to the incident discovered on November 8 and that a full technical report on the exploit will follow.

Operators are advised to set up a fresh server if compromise is suspected, because updating alone cannot remove access an attacker may already have gained. Additional recovery steps include rotating all user wallet keys as Super Admin, replacing funding-source credentials (API keys or macaroons), rotating other secrets stored in settings, and reviewing or re-creating connections for extensions such as NWC Provider, LNDHub, Bolt Cards, TPOS, Bleskomat, and FOSSA.

The GitHub release notes describe extensive security hardening across authentication, extension management, and payment handling, plus dependency updates. New features shipping with the version include on-chain wallet support, BOLT12 offer payments, TOTP two-factor authentication, NIP-17 support, a job scheduler, automatic extension updates, and hold invoice support for the LNbits funding source. The project notes that LNbits functions as a hot wallet and that operators should keep only operational balances online, regularly review activity, and move excess funds to offline keys.

LNbits was founded as a free and open-source project in 2019 by Ben Arc and later incorporated a commercial entity to support development. It provides multi-user accounts and a large suite of extensions on top of any Lightning funding source (including LND and Core Lightning). Bitcoin Magazine profiled the project in September 2024, describing it as “the WordPress for your Bitcoin Lightning node” noting its widepsread use by individuals, businesses, and node operators seeking accessible Lightning tooling. The software is widely deployed both self-hosted and via managed instances.

As of the v1.7.0 announcement, the project has not published an aggregate total of funds lost across all affected instances. Operators who have not yet upgraded are directed to the official guidance before bringing servers back online.

#5045. Moody’s Taps Chainlink to Send Credit Ratings Across Multiple Blockchains

Moody’s Taps Chainlink to Send Credit Ratings Across Multiple Blockchains

Chainlink and Moody’s Ratings have completed a pilot showing how credit ratings could be beamed across multiple blockchains at once, another sign that the traditional machinery of finance is steadily moving onchain.

Announced Tuesday, the test used Chainlink’s Cross-Chain Interoperability Protocol, or CCIP, to distribute Moody’s ratings simultaneously across both permissioned and permissionless blockchain networks.

It ran through the Moody’s Ratings Token Integration Engine, or TIE, the system the 125-year-old ratings giant built to disseminate credit assessments and related data within blockchain environments. The pilot, the companies said, preserved Moody’s existing publication and governance processes while proving the ratings could travel across chains through a single interoperable layer.

The logic is straightforward: as more bonds, funds, and other assets are issued as blockchain tokens, the investors trading them will expect the credit intelligence they rely on to be available wherever those assets live, rather than stopping at any one network’s edge.

Moody’s brings real experience here, having rated or assessed more than 70 digital issuances, including digital bonds and tokenized funds, between January 2017 and October 2026.

“As more financial assets move onchain, credit intelligence has to move with them, across chains and through every rating change,” said Johann Eid, chief business officer at Chainlink Labs, calling the pilot a foundational piece of what institutional capital needs to adopt tokenization at scale.

Fabian Astic, Moody’s global head of digital economy, argued that “the future of tokenized finance is unlikely to reside on a single blockchain, platform, or market infrastructure,” and that investors will increasingly expect ratings regardless of the underlying technology.

The effort builds on Moody’s steady push onchain. Earlier this year, the firm launched TIE on the institutional Canton Network before extending it to Solana, making its ratings machine-readable on a major public blockchain.

Chainlink, meanwhile, has positioned CCIP as the connective tissue for institutional tokenization, recently rolling out an upgraded version and reporting tens of billions of dollars in cross-chain value secured as banks and asset managers experiment with moving assets between networks.

The pilot, though, ran entirely on a testnet using representative test data, with no actual rating actions published onchain and no live dissemination involved.

It demonstrated technical capability rather than a launched product, but the companies said the model is designed to accommodate future rating updates under appropriate governance. For now, it’s a proof of concept, albeit one pointing toward a future where even credit ratings are just another piece of data flowing across blockchains.

#5043. Bitcoin Heated Hot Tubs and 278 Washington Bitcoin Merchants : Now Accepting Bitcoin

Washington Bitcoin merchant list has 278 businesses, from coffee shops and salons to a hot tub maker that heats its tubs with bitcoin mining. The list spans 94 named cities, and Seattle leads the way with 46 listings. First up is a homegrown US manufacturing business from Washington that makes a super unique product.

Snorkel has been making hot tubs in the Pacific Northwest since 1979, and its wood-stove business dates to the late 1970s. What makes them special is their full cedar tubs, and the wood stove which is fully submerged into the water of the cedar hot tub and has the “snorkel” coming out of the water. This is very convenient for anyone who has no electrical hook up.

It started with a bet in 2025. Michael Diederich, the owner, began accepting bitcoin for hot tubs after a wager with his brother. Payments were slow to trickle in at first, but bitcoiners kept asking him to build a hot tub powered by bitcoin miners. He started building one, pairing the tub with miners that heat the water. Snorkel works with Dane Soja of Hashrate House on the build.

The prototype with two S19J Pro miners drawing about 8 kW, which it says heated a tub from cold to 104°F (40°C) in under four hours. The project won an innovation award at the Heat Punk Summit in Denver, and a Snorkel tub is on display in Denver. Diederich sees a future where every electric hot tub is heated by miners, because the mining income helps pay back the cost of energy that would otherwise be wasted as heat.

The Rose sits at 226 Main Ave S in downtown Renton, in the Old Renton Pub building. Owner Katie Newman opened it in October 2020 as a gift shop that happened to serve coffee, and the coffee side grew into the larger part of the business. The shop stocks locally roasted coffee, handpicked artisan goods, and grab-and-go food. It won Best Customer Service in the 2025 Best of Renton readers’ poll.

Overcast Coffee is on Capitol Hill, and it started as a walk-up counter at 312 15th Ave E beside Kaiser Permanente. Owner Jonathan Pak opened the first shop there in June 2020. A second location, at 1517 12th Ave inside the Metier bike complex, came later, and the company buys its coffee and ingredients from small, local Seattle businesses.

#5040. Initia confirms second exploit in 3 weeks, $3.27M stolen in latest attack

Initia said an attacker exploited compromised cloud credentials to move $3.27 million in crypto assets, with about $2.16 million remaining frozen on its halted network.

Layer-1 blockchain network Initia confirmed Wednesday that it has suffered its second security exploit in less than three weeks, with an attacker stealing about $3.27 million in crypto assets after compromising the platform’s cloud infrastructure.

On Wednesday, Initia said the attacker gained unauthorized access on Monday through a command injection attack involving a compromised Google Cloud Platform key. The company said the breach exposed environment variables, allowing the attacker to access Initia’s GitHub and cloud accounts and obtain additional credentials.

According to Initia, the attacker accessed Anvil, a platform hosting rollups, and validated fraudulent Inter-Blockchain Communication (IBC) withdrawals from five rollups, releasing assets held in the network’s escrow accounts.

Initia said about $2.16 million of the stolen assets remain frozen on its halted network. The attacker sold assets on Initia’s decentralized exchange and withdrew 13,400 TIA, 8,000 USDC.inj and 597,000 Noble USDC. No INIT tokens were withdrawn.

Initia halted its layer-1 on Tuesday and said it intends to restore the backing of affected assets. The blockchain said recovery efforts are ongoing with its law enforcement and security partners.

The incident follows an Oct. 23 exploit that saw an attacker drain about $2.7 million after exploiting a vulnerability in Initia’s MoveVM script cache. This allowed malicious code to bypass security checks and create unbacked token balances that were subsequently bridged out of the network.

#5038. Anthropic Sets IPO Price Range at $70 to $78, Valuing Company at Up to $1.4 Trillion

Anthropic Sets IPO Price Range at $70 to $78, Valuing Company at Up to $1.4 Trillion

Anthropic has set a price range of $70 to $78 per share for its initial public offering, according to an updated filing with the Securities and Exchange Commission on Tuesday.

The AI company plans to sell 825 million shares of Class A common stock, 801.4 million of them new shares and 23.6 million from existing shareholders, on the Nasdaq under the ticker "ANT," which it revealed when it made its IPO filing public last month. At the top of the range, the offering would raise about $64.4 billion, including the selling shareholders' stake. Underwriters have an option to buy a further 123.75 million shares.

Anthropic expects to price the offering on November 18 and begin trading on November 19, and is targeting a retail allocation of about 20%, well above the norm for an IPO, CNBC reported, citing people familiar with the details.

Based on the roughly 13.09 billion shares the filing says will be outstanding after the offering, the range implies a market value of about $916 billion to $1.02 trillion. On a fully diluted basis, the company could be worth up to $1.405 trillion according to CNBC, up from the $965 billion valuation it announced in a funding round in May.

Alongside the IPO, seven of the companies that supply Anthropic's computing power have agreed to buy a combined $26 billion of Class N common stock, which has no voting rights, at the IPO price. At the midpoint of the range, Google and Nvidia would each buy about $10 billion, AMD about $2.5 billion, Broadcom, Micron and Microsoft about $1 billion each, and TSMC about $500 million, according to the filing. Those shares will not be subject to the usual post-IPO lock-up period.

Anthropic expects net proceeds of about $84.6 billion from the IPO and the private placement combined at the midpoint, which it plans to use for general corporate purposes, including investment in compute and infrastructure, according to the filing.

The company reported revenue of $33.2 billion for the first nine months of 2026, up from $2.5 billion a year earlier. It posted a net loss of $104 billion over the period, most of it from a $94.3 billion non-cash charge tied to the rising value of its convertible notes and other instruments; its operating loss was $1.26 billion. Anthropic has also committed to about $900.9 billion of spending, mostly on compute and infrastructure, of which about $70.7 billion is due within the next 12 months.

Anthropic will list as a public benefit corporation with an unusual governance structure. Its Long-Term Benefit Trust, a body of financially disinterested trustees, will elect a majority of its board, while a single share of Class F stock held by an entity owned by its seven co-founders will carry 50.1% of the company's voting power on most matters, according to the filing. As a result, Anthropic will be a "controlled company" under Nasdaq rules, and holders of its Class A shares will have limited influence over major decisions, the filing warns.

The range comes after a turbulent week for AI stocks. Some major investors told The Wall Street Journal last week that Anthropic's expected $1.5 trillion valuation would likely need to come down following a sell-off in AI stocks, with one suggesting a figure closer to $1 trillion. Anthropic had been eyeing a valuation of around $2 trillion as recently as September, according to an earlier draft filing.

The IPO's order book is already covered at the proposed range, CNBC reported, citing people familiar with the matter.

#5035. SEC enforcement actions fall 24%, crypto cases in spotlight

The SEC filed 348 actions in fiscal 2026, down from 456 a year earlier. Cases included alleged Bitcoin mining fraud and an FTX audit settlement.

The US Securities and Exchange Commission (SEC) filed 348 enforcement actions in fiscal 2026, down 23.7% from 456 a year earlier.

Its annual enforcement results included cases involving alleged crypto fraud and failures in FTX’s audits.

In its Tuesday release, the SEC said it had charged three purported crypto trading platforms and four investment clubs in an alleged scheme that defrauded investors of at least $14 million. In a separate case, the agency accused the founder of a Bitcoin mining business of misappropriating $48.5 million after raising more than $95.6 million from about 6,400 investors.

In April, the SEC settled charges against Francis Decker, the Prager Metis partner who led FTX’s audits, over failures to meet professional auditing standards.

In February, a jury found Ismael Zarco Sanchez liable for securities fraud and registration violations over his role in the $300 million CryptoFX Ponzi scheme. In July, a court ordered Michael Noble to pay $1.07 million in a case over his role promoting BitConnect’s crypto lending program.

Fiscal 2026 was SEC Chair Paul Atkins’ first full fiscal year in office. The 348 enforcement actions were the agency’s fewest since 1991, according to a Reuters review of SEC records. Standalone actions fell to 235 from 303 a year earlier.

The decline in crypto enforcement was already visible in 2025, when the SEC filed 13 crypto-related actions, down from 33 in 2024, according to Cornerstone Research. Five of the 13 were filed before former Chair Gary Gensler left office in January 2025.

The SEC said its new enforcement analytics office was using “blockchain and financial-flow analysis” to detect potential misconduct earlier.

Related: SEC flags tokenization as high-risk area in 2027 market examinations

#5032. Moody’s and Chainlink test cross-chain credit ratings distribution

Looking for institutional-grade crypto trading? Discover LMAX Digital, the regulated exchange from LMAX Group

This was a testnet demonstration using representative data, with no actual ratings published onchain or live dissemination processes involved.

Moody's Ratings and Chainlink completed a pilot demonstrating the cross-chain distribution of credit ratings across multiple blockchain networks using Chainlink's Cross-Chain Interoperability Protocol (CCIP).

The testnet pilot used Moody’s Token Integration Engine to simultaneously distribute credit insights across permissioned and permissionless networks, demonstrating how ratings information could be made available to tokenized assets across different blockchains while preserving Moody’s existing publication and governance processes.

Earlier this year, Moody's launched a token engine to bring its credit analysis onchain, starting with the Canton Network.

"The future of tokenized finance is unlikely to reside on a single blockchain, platform, or market infrastructure," said Fabian Astic, Global Head of Digital Economy at Moody's. "As digital markets continue to evolve, investors will increasingly expect access to credit ratings and associated information regardless of the underlying technology supporting an asset."

The release explicitly says this was a testnet demonstration using representative data, with no actual ratings published onchain or live dissemination processes involved.

"This pilot explored how interoperable infrastructure can help make credit ratings available across multiple blockchain networks while preserving the governance and publication processes that support confidence in our analytical opinions," Astic said.

Between January 2017 and October 2026, Moody's Ratings rated or assessed more than 70 digital issuances, including digital bonds and tokenized funds.

Of note, Chainlink recently expanded its collaboration with S&P Global Ratings to bring vault risk assessments onchain.

US spot Bitcoin ETFs have lost $1.6 billion in November, while stock funds attracted billions of dollars from investors buying the dip.

US spot Bitcoin exchange-traded funds (ETFs) recorded $95 million in net outflows on Tuesday as investors poured billions of dollars into stock funds during a market pullback.

Tuesday’s withdrawals brought net outflows from US spot Bitcoin (BTC) ETFs to roughly $1.6 billion since the start of November, according to SoSoValue data.

Bitcoin ETF outflows eased for a third straight session, while stock funds accounted for all 17 of the top US-listed ETFs by inflows over five trading sessions, according to Bloomberg ETF analyst Eric Balchunas.

US-listed ETFs attracted roughly $12 billion per day over those five sessions, with all 17 of the top funds by inflows investing in equities, Balchunas said on X on Tuesday.

US-listed ETFs ranked by one-week net inflows, led by equity funds. Source: Eric Balchunas

Morgan Stanley’s Bitcoin Trust ETF (MSBT) attracted $20 million Tuesday, although the inflows were insufficient to offset broader losses.

Daily net outflows from Bitcoin ETFs outflows slowed from $430 million Thursday and $412 million Friday to $201 million Monday and $95 million Tuesday, but broader market indicators remained weak, according to CryptoQuant.

CryptoQuant’s Bitcoin Bull Score Index dropped to 20 on Monday from 90 on Oct. 13, signaling “extremely bearish” conditions, according to a Tuesday report seen by Cointelegraph.

#5028. MEGA commits $30 million to new products, starting with AI inference

Looking for institutional-grade crypto trading? Discover LMAX Digital, the regulated exchange from LMAX Group

The MegaETH Foundation rebrands as MEGA and commits $30 million to new products, with an AI inference market set to launch in December.

The MegaETH Foundation, the team behind the Ethereum Layer 2 network MegaETH, has rebranded as MEGA and committed $30 million to new products, starting with an AI inference market planned for December.

The announcement comes after months of work in stealth mode, MEGA said Tuesday, adding that the team will now also develop new products alongside the MegaETH network.

All new products will run on MegaETH, MEGA said. Planned network upgrades, including fast finality, will also help support its AI inference product.

Inference is the process of running a trained AI model to produce an output. MEGA said providers charge different prices for similar services, but buyers may find it hard to know the risks of using cheaper providers they are not familiar with.

"There are still huge gaps between what providers charge for comparable inference," MegaETH co-founder Yilong Li said in a statement. "We think those gaps can be dramatically reduced."

MEGA calls its approach "Purified Intelligence," aiming to make inference from different providers more interchangeable, allowing more suppliers to compete in a global market.

Asked how the market would work and which providers or models would be available, MegaETH co-founder Shuyao Kong told The Block that MEGA had intentionally held back those details until the December launch.

"We believe crypto is the only technology that makes AI fair and open," she said, adding that developers will be the target customers for the inference market.

The $30 million will come from MEGA's existing treasury to fund a range of products, Kong said, noting inference as the project's "largest move."

MEGA said its mandate includes expanding the uses of the MEGA token. When asked what role the token would have in the inference market, Kong said: "This is our mandate, so wherever there are ways to utilize the MEGA token, we will."

The MegaETH team has raised more than $80 million to date across venture funding, community rounds and token sales. That includes a $20 million seed round announced in June 2024, led by Dragonfly with backing from Vitalik Buterin, Joseph Lubin and others; a $10 million Echo round in December 2024; about $13 million from its Fluffle NFT sale in February 2025; and about $40 million from its public token sale in October 2025, according to Kong. She said the public sale amount reflects discounts for buyers who agreed to lock their tokens for one year.

Kong also noted that MegaETH bought back some investors' stakes last year. As The Block previously reported, the team repurchased a 4.75% stake comprising equity and token warrants from pre-seed investors in summer 2025.

The MegaETH network launched its public mainnet earlier this year and currently has about $12 million in total value locked, down from its peak of over $245 million, according to DefiLlama data.

The MEGA token went live in April this year and is currently trading at around $0.05, down over 76% from its all-time high, according to The Block's MEGA price page.

#5027. Solana's Jupiter Expands Beyond Trading With Token Baskets, Yield Vaults and New Portfolio

Solana's Jupiter Expands Beyond Trading With Token Baskets, Yield Vaults and New Portfolio

Jupiter, the largest trading platform on Solana, is rolling out four new products this week as it aims to expand from a place to trade tokens into an all-in-one home for onchain assets.

The company announced a new mission on Tuesday, "to make the world's assets universally accessible and useful," alongside the releases, which cover discovering, owning, earning on and managing assets.

Jupiter built its business by aggregating liquidity from across Solana so that each trade gets the best available price. It says that approach has processed more than $1.3 trillion in cumulative aggregator volume, and that its wider ecosystem now holds about $1.7 billion in total value locked, according to DeFiLlama data from early November. Its lending market has passed $1 billion in active loans, the company said.

"What Google did for information, Jupiter is doing for assets," said Kash Dhanda, Jupiter's chief operating officer. "We started with the trade because it was the hardest problem on Solana, and aggregation solved it. That taught us something: aggregation doesn't just work for trading, it works for everything."

Dhanda has framed the goal in even bigger terms. "I'm a big believer that the era of do-it-yourself DeFi is over," he said in a livestreamed interview with Incrypted on October 29, recalling having "12 tabs open and a spreadsheet" to manage positions across different venues. He said he hopes Jupiter "in five years' time will be the only money app that you need to use."

Baskets let users buy a bundle of tokens with a single SOL or USDC deposit instead of making a separate swap for each one. Baskets can follow market-cap weights or fixed targets and rebalance automatically, with every deposit, rebalance and exit routed through Jupiter for the best available price.

Jupiter Vaults is a new hub for yield strategies, from stablecoin lending to strategies backed by real-world assets, managed by independent curators rather than Jupiter itself. Users can review each strategy's risk profile, allocation and historical returns before depositing. Vaults opens on November 11 as a private beta for more than 19,000 people on its waitlist and eligible holders of staked JUP.

In the October interview, Dhanda said Jupiter's yield products are audited multiple times before reaching users. Asked what would happen if a product were exploited, he said that "especially if it's our fault, we'd like to work with users to find a good solution," and that Jupiter is exploring ways to insure such products onchain.

Portfolio V3, live now, turns Jupiter's portfolio page into a place to act on positions, with quick actions to trade, open perpetual futures, make predictions or lend, and alerts for positions near liquidation. It also adds cross-chain transfers that combine several bridges.

Pulse and rebuilt token pages are meant to make Jupiter a place to research assets as well as trade them, showing market data for tokenized stocks and ETFs, tokenomics for DeFi projects, and a sidebar for comparing tokens. Dhanda has argued that putting assets onchain is only half the job. "People talk about tokenization as if it is the end game, when in reality, it is just the first step of the game," he said in October.

The releases will roll out over the coming days, with Jupiter sharing details of each one before Solana Breakpoint, the network's annual conference, which starts on November 15.

All Vaults strategies carry risk, including the possible loss of funds, and some may not be available in all regions, including the United States, Jupiter said.

Jupiter's launches come amid a flurry of activity across the Solana ecosystem ahead of the conference. Last week, Solana Labs' new company Leap launched a beta mobile app for trading tokenized stocks, ETFs, metals, currencies and crypto in one place.

The network has also been courting institutions. Last month, the Solana Foundation unveiled an open standard for institutional settlement developed with input from J.P. Morgan, while Polygon Labs extended its Open Money Stack, a suite of stablecoin payment tools linking bank accounts, wallets and payouts, to Solana.

Under the hood, Solana completed its biggest consensus upgrade since its 2020 launch in October, when Alpenglow went live and cut the time for a transaction to become irreversible from about 12.8 seconds to roughly 150 milliseconds. Solana treasury firm DeFi Development Corp has also added $3 million worth of SOL to its holdings.

Jupiter powers token swaps on The Information Exchange, Decrypt's trading platform.

Bitcoin Bitcoin (BTC) mining hardware maker Canaan Canaan Inc. (CAN) sold 1 million TH/s of computing power in the third quarter, bringing in $4.8 million from mining equipment, down 96% from $118.6 million in the same period last year.

Canaan's overall revenue fell 90% to $14.5 million from $150.5 million a year earlier, according to its financial results published Tuesday. Canaan attributed the drop in hardware revenue to lower computing power sold and weaker average selling prices amid reduced market demand.

Canaan Chief Financial Officer Jin "James" Cheng attributed the gains to bitcoin's higher price at the end of September. "We recognize that this non-cash gain is distinct from underlying operating performance," he said in the release.

ERCOT classified Canaan's proposed 1 GW Duval County power project as "Studied Load – Firm" on a conditional basis, and further studies and approvals are still pending. Its second 1 GW Texas project, in Zapata County, remains in early development, the firm said.

Of the approximately 1,875 BTC Canaan held at the end of September, 1,258 BTC was pledged for secured loans. The company also announced a $20 million share buyback program under a $100 million authorization approved by its board on Nov. 6.

#5023. ECB warns legislative delays could postpone digital euro launch

ECB Executive Board member Piero Cipollone warned EU lawmakers that delays in finalizing digital euro legislation could push back its potential 2029 rollout.

European Central Bank (ECB) Executive Board member Piero Cipollone called for European Union negotiators to reach a deal on digital euro legislation by year-end to keep a possible 2029 launch on track.

Speaking at the European Parliament’s Committee on Economic and Monetary Affairs on Tuesday, Cipollone said adoption of the regulation, part of the EU’s Single Currency Package, would give the ECB the legal certainty it needs to complete preparations.

“A delay in adoption could instead postpone the introduction of the digital euro,” Cipollone said, warning that a later launch could deepen Europe’s dependence on foreign payment providers and make it harder for European companies to compete.

The ECB aims to be ready for a potential first issuance in 2029 if the EU adopts the regulation this year. Its Governing Council will decide whether and when to issue the digital euro after the legislation passes.

The European Parliament’s economic affairs committee approved its position on the digital euro legislation in June in a 43–14 vote. Parliament adopted its negotiating position in July, clearing the way for talks with EU member states.

The ECB is preparing a 12-month pilot set to begin in the second half of 2027. Payment providers and selected merchants will test a beta version of the digital euro.

The warning comes as the ECB prepares for a leadership change. President Christine Lagarde announced in October that she would step down on Feb. 7, 2027, ahead of the planned pilot.

#5022. Bitcoin Falls as Hot US Inflation Fuels Fears of Bigger Fed Rate Hike

Bitcoin Falls as Hot US Inflation Fuels Fears of Bigger Fed Rate Hike

Bitcoin fell below $74,000 on Tuesday after U.S. inflation came in hotter than expected for the third consecutive month, adding fresh pressure to a crypto market already struggling with falling prices and investor withdrawals.

The Consumer Price Index rose 0.4% in October, above economists' forecasts of 0.3%, according to data released by the Bureau of Labor Statistics. Core CPI, which strips out food and energy prices, climbed 0.3% against expectations of 0.2%, matching September's reading for the third consecutive month.

Headline inflation actually slowed from September's 0.6% increase, but that offered little relief to traders who had expected a bigger slowdown. Both figures exceeded forecasts by 0.1 percentage points, giving the Federal Reserve another reason to keep interest rates elevated.

Bitcoin was trading near $74,200 before the announcement and briefly dropped to approximately $73,525 immediately afterward. The cryptocurrency recovered some ground toward $74,000 but remained under pressure, trading roughly 1% lower over 24 hours.

Ethereum also weakened, slipping toward $2,040 during the initial selloff, while other major altcoins faced selling pressure. Approximately $178 million in crypto long positions were liquidated within an hour of the announcement.

Liquidations happen when exchanges automatically close trades made with borrowed money because their owners no longer have enough collateral to cover potential losses. Those forced sales can push prices even lower, triggering further liquidations.

Bitcoin was already having a difficult week. The asset had lost roughly 4.5% over seven days, while U.S. spot Bitcoin ETFs recorded another $201 million in net withdrawals on Monday, extending the selling pressure that preceded the inflation report.

Higher inflation usually means higher interest rates, and higher interest rates tend to be bad news for crypto. When government bonds and cash offer attractive returns, investors have less incentive to hold volatile assets such as Bitcoin, which generate no interest simply by being held.

Higher borrowing costs also reduce the availability of cheap money for speculative investments. That's why Bitcoin, despite its reputation as an inflation hedge, frequently falls when inflation data suggests the Fed will tighten monetary policy.

The latest report landed just hours after Fed Governor Christopher Waller made unusually aggressive comments about the central bank's next move.

Speaking in London, Waller said he would support a quarter-percentage-point interest rate increase in December even if inflation improved. But he also warned that another core CPI reading of 0.3% could justify a larger move.

“I believe a 50 basis point hike in the policy rate should be considered at the December meeting,” he said.

October's reading hit precisely that threshold. According to Reuters, financial analysts increased their estimated probability of a 50-basis-point hike to nearly 15% by midmorning, although a smaller quarter-point increase remained the overwhelming favorite.

For context, the Fed currently holds its benchmark interest rate between 3.75% and 4%. A 50-basis-point increase would lift that range to 4.25%-4.50%, making borrowing more expensive across all of the economy.

Bond markets reacted accordingly. The two-year Treasury yield, which closely tracks expectations for Fed policy, posted its biggest increase since April 2025, while the dollar strengthened and U.S. stocks opened lower.

October's inflation report offered a mixed picture of the American economy. Food prices rose 0.5%, energy increased 0.7%, and shelter costs climbed 0.3%, accounting for more than 30% of the monthly headline increase.

Energy inflation slowed considerably from September's 5.2% surge, but price increases remained widespread across services. Recreation costs climbed 0.8%, motor vehicle insurance rose 0.7%, and medical care increased 0.3%.

The report also came with an unusual limitation: The government did not publish its standard annual inflation rate because the 2025 federal shutdown prevented the collection of October data that year. September's last available annual headline reading stood at 3.7%, well above the Fed's 2% inflation target, which is formally measured using a separate index known as Personal Consumption Expenditures.

Crypto investors have seen this pattern before. In October, Bitcoin fell after September's hotter-than-expected inflation report, and the selloff continued as rate-hike expectations strengthened.

This time, the market enters the next Fed meeting with Bitcoin substantially below its October levels and institutional demand showing signs of weakness. Higher Treasury yields could make a sustained recovery more difficult unless inflation cools or investors regain their appetite for risk.

The Fed's final policy meeting of 2026 is scheduled for December 8-9. The next CPI report will arrive on December 10, meaning policymakers will have to make their December rate decision without seeing November's consumer inflation figures.

#5016. Goldman, Citi and Global Banks Commit $270 Million to Challenge Crypto’s Stablecoin Giants

Goldman, Citi and Global Banks Commit $270 Million to Challenge Crypto’s Stablecoin Giants

The group of global banks building a joint stablecoin has put real money on the table, committing around $270 million to fund the venture as it moves from blueprint to buildout.

Goldman Sachs, Citigroup, Banco Santander, and Deutsche Bank are among the institutions backing the effort, Bloomberg reported Tuesday, citing people familiar with the matter.

The capital is meant to cover five years of operations for a planned U.S.-incorporated company that will issue a dollar-denominated stablecoin. Known among participants as Project Faro, the venture is still searching for a chief executive, and its final name remains under discussion.

A spokesperson for the project declined to comment, as did Santander and Deutsche Bank, while Goldman and Citi referred questions to the project.

The funding is the concrete next step for an initiative that first went public late last year. As Decrypt previously reported, 21 of the world’s largest financial institutions committed in September to form a company to issue a U.S. dollar stablecoin, targeting a launch in the first half of 2027, with a euro version and other currencies to follow.

That consortium had more than doubled from an initial group of 10 banks that began exploring the idea in October 2025, and now spans North America, Europe, East Asia, the Middle East and Africa.

Beyond the four named by Bloomberg, its participants include Bank of America, Wells Fargo, Fidelity Investments, PNC, Capital One, TD Bank, UBS, BBVA, Deutsche Bank, Lloyds Banking Group and MUFG, among others. JPMorgan, notably, is not on the list.

The venture is one of several collaborative pushes by established finance into a business long dominated by crypto-native players. Visa, Mastercard and Stripe are among more than 100 companies backing the issuer Open Standard, while European banks have formed Qivalis to issue a euro-denominated coin.

At the moment, Tether’s USDT and Circle’s USDC together account for about 85% of a market worth roughly $290 billion, nearly all of it dollar-pegged. When the bank consortium first went public in September, Circle’s stock fell about 6% as investors priced in the prospect of bank-backed competition for USDC.

The banks are betting that institutional trust, regulatory alignment and existing client networks can win them a share of a settlement business that promises round-the-clock, cross-border payments at lower cost than traditional rails.

The token is being designed to comply with the U.S. GENIUS Act, which created the first federal framework for dollar-pegged tokens in 2025, and, where applicable, the EU’s MiCA regime. The group has said it wants the stablecoin used across wholesale, institutional and retail markets.

For now, Project Faro remains pre-launch, with no issued token, no named CEO, not even a final name, and a 2027 target that leaves ample room for delay.

But the $270 million commitment marks a shift, and a concrete signal that this bank consortium intends to compete for the onchain dollar rather than leave it to crypto.

November has historically been BTC's strongest month, with an average return of 34.4% and a median gain of 8.6% since 2010.

#5005. Elon Musk Calls Claude Opus 5.5 a 'Masterpiece', Admits Grok 4.8 Won't Match It

Elon Musk Calls Claude Opus 5.5 a 'Masterpiece', Admits Grok 4.8 Won't Match It

Elon Musk has given a rival AI model an unusually enthusiastic endorsement while acknowledging that his own next release won't be able to beat it. Asked how Grok 4.8 would compare with Anthropic's Claude Opus 5.5, the xAI founder answered it would be “not as good.”

“Opus 5.5 is a masterpiece,” Musk posted on X.

The admission came in a reply on X Tuesday to a question from a user named Dimdv. Musk added that Grok 5, a next generation model that will be released after this update, “should exceed Opus 5.5,” potentially in two or three months, although he gave further details for that prediction or a firm date for its release—and he’s known for not sticking to his own target dates.

For a company trying to catch up with Anthropic and OpenAI, the distinction matters. Grok 4.8 is the update Musk expects to put in some users' hands this week; Grok 5 is the model he believes could eventually put his company ahead, until competing AI companies release a new model.

The exchange began with a technical update in which Musk said Grok 4.8 had finished pretraining but was still undergoing reinforcement learning, or RL, a phase in which a model's responses are refined using feedback and rewards. He said the team had also spent considerable time cleaning up supervised fine-tuning data, the examples used to teach the model how to follow instructions.

Musk said the company had abandoned JAX, a machine-learning framework, and SGLang, software used to serve AI models, in favor of an inference and reinforcement-learning system rewritten in C and C++. Inference is the process of generating answers after a user sends a prompt; rewriting the underlying software can offer engineers more control over performance, though Musk provided no figures showing the resulting gains.

Developer tetsuo celebrated the announcement with “C wins again,” prompting Musk to clarify that the code was “C with a little C++.” He subsequently credited Starlink engineers with work that helped produce the Grok training and inference stack, pointing to SpaceX's practice of writing its own headers and handling precise time calculations, including leap seconds.

Musk described Grok 4.8 as “not yet incredible” but said its foundations were finally built correctly. He expects a soft rollout to Bot users later this week, giving the company an early test before any broader release.

Anthropic released Opus 5.5 on September 22, delivering a performance comparable to its higher-end Fable 5.1 model on most work while costing 40% less to run than the previous Opus version on typical workloads. The model targets difficult programming jobs and tasks in which AI agents work through multiple steps with limited human assistance.

On Terminal-Bench 4.0, which tests whether AI agents can complete complicated tasks in a command-line environment and scores them by the percentage successfully finished, Anthropic reported 66.4% for Opus 5.5 versus 57.9% for OpenAI's GPT-6 Astra. Grok is very far behind in the leaderboards.

Musk has not published Grok 4.8 scores that would establish how large the gap really is. His immediate target is the limited rollout later this week; the promised Grok 5 challenge to Opus 5.5 is still, by his own estimate, two or three months away.

#5002. Bitget Wallet reports sevenfold jump in crypto phishing risk alerts

The wallet flagged about 37,000 signing requests a week for phishing in Q3 and said better monitoring likely contributed to the increase.

Crypto wallet provider Bitget Wallet said Wednesday it was expanding AI-assisted threat detection after its weekly average of signing requests flagged for potential phishing rose 7.4-fold in the third quarter of 2026.

The wallet flagged about 37,000 such requests per week in Q3, up from 5,000 in Q2, according to Bitget Wallet chief operating officer Alvin Kan.

“The increase likely reflects both greater threat activity and improved detection capabilities, although the data does not allow us to quantify each factor’s contribution,” Kan told Cointelegraph.

The wallet introduced AI-assisted risk detection in Q2, he added.

Kan said that the tally counted flagged signing interactions, not unique attacks or confirmed financial losses.

Bitget Wallet said its expanded AI checks examine website code and domain registration details for signs of fraud. The wallet also simulates transactions to spot hidden permissions before users sign.

Bitget Wallet also said AI helped identify 90% of newly blocked risky domains in October, compared with 36% in Q2. Nearly 70% of domains its AI flagged that month had no matching alerts in the external threat feeds it checked.

The findings come amid continued phishing threats targeting crypto users. In July, a trader lost nearly $1 million after signing a malicious Ethereum token approval.

An August investigation by Rapid7 uncovered a phishing campaign that targeted 885,000 phone numbers and directed recipients to websites impersonating crypto wallet providers.

#4998. Near AI Breach Exposes User Details and Data From 18 AI Agents

Near AI Breach Exposes User Details and Data From 18 AI Agents

Near AI has disclosed a breach that exposed account information belonging to about 100 users and data from 18 AI agents after an attacker exploited software running its servers.

The company said in a November 9 report that an attacker used a remote code execution vulnerability in SGLang, an open-source engine that runs AI models and generates responses to user requests.

The exploit allowed the intruder to execute commands on one inference server and then reach other parts of Near AI’s infrastructure.

Investigators traced the intrusion to an internal system connected to Private Chat, a feature the company had already retired. The attacker downloaded names and email addresses for approximately 100 users, along with subscription plan and account balance information for some of them.

Another 18 agents were accessed on an older deployment of Near AI Agents, where the attacker retrieved their chat histories, settings and logs. Those agents were not running inside NEAR AI’s confidential computing environment, which is designed to shield information from the company operating the servers.

Logs also exposed NEAR AI Cloud API keys belonging to two customers. These keys function like digital credentials that applications use to access cloud services; Near AI says it has revoked both, but did not say whether the attacker used them.

Near AI found no indication that stored Private Chat conversations or uploaded files had been accessed. The confirmed theft involved account records and legacy agent data, while exposure of live confidential requests remains a possibility identified by the investigation.

Hardware isolation does not guarantee that the software inside the protected environment is safe. “It does not protect against a vulnerability in the software running inside the environment,” Near AI acknowledged, explaining that the SGLang flaw let the attacker run code where confidential requests were being processed.

SGLang had already attracted scrutiny from security researchers. In July, Carnegie Mellon University’s CERT Coordination Center documented six vulnerabilities in the project, including bugs that could let attackers execute commands or expose credentials, depending on how servers were configured.

Near AI did not identify the specific SGLang flaw exploited in this incident, so there is no basis to link the breach to any of those earlier disclosures. It reported the vulnerability to SGLang’s developers.

Near, to its credit, was quick to fix the issues.

“On detection, we revoked all active sessions and administrative credentials, replaced all agent tokens, took Private Chat offline, and deployed a fix for the SGLang vulnerability across all inference servers,” the project said.

”We've completed the rotation of all remaining credentials and a full forensic review. We have notified users whose account details or agents we have confirmed were accessed and will make any regulatory notifications that are required.”

Near AI notified users whose account records or agents it confirmed were accessed and says it will make any required regulatory notifications. It also warned users to be wary of unsolicited messages referring to their Near AI accounts or balances, and contacted the owners of all 18 affected agents individually.

#4996. New York federal judge denies Novig’s injunction bid, rules its sports event contracts are not swaps

Looking for institutional-grade crypto trading? Discover LMAX Digital, the regulated exchange from LMAX Group

The decision allows the state to require Novig either to obtain a state gambling license or to prohibit the listing and trading of sports event contracts on its platform.

A federal judge in New York denied a preliminary injunction sought by sports prediction market platform Novig, ruling that its sports-related event contracts do not qualify as swaps under the Commodity Exchange Act.

According to a Tuesday court filing, U.S. District Judge Colleen McMahon of the Southern District of New York concluded that the state may apply its gambling and sports-wagering laws to Novig’s activity. The decision allows New York to require Novig either to obtain a state gambling license or prohibit the listing and trading of sports event contracts on its platform.

Ludlow Exchange, LLC, the legal entity behind Novig, received the Commodity Futures Trading Commission's designation as a contract market in June 2026 and began offering sports event contracts to New York customers in August.

On Aug. 5, 2026, the company sued New York Attorney General Letitia James and officials at the New York State Gaming Commission, arguing that federal commodities law preempts state regulation of its products. It contended that the CFTC has "exclusive jurisdiction" over event contracts, which qualify as swaps under the act.

Judge McMahon rejected that argument at the preliminary injunction stage. She wrote that Novig’s sports event contracts “bear more than a passing resemblance to sports betting” and fall outside the CEA’s definition of a swap.

"The Court concludes that Novig’s contracts do not depend on events or contingencies that are ‘associated with a potential financial, economic, or commercial consequence’ within the meaning of § 1a(47)(A)(ii). Accordingly, they are not ‘swaps’ under the CEA," the filing said.

The ruling follows a similar decision earlier this year by another New York federal judge that denied Kalshi's request to block state authorities from enforcing gambling laws on its prediction market platform. Novig is also litigating the same issue in multiple other states, as Kalshi, Polymarket, and other prediction market platforms are doing.

"NY Attorney General Letitia James could literally cross the street and file a state-court civil enforcement action against Novig as soon as today," sports betting and gaming lawyer Daniel Wallach wrote on X.

Wallach added that he's seeing a larger shift where state regulators may move from defending their enforcement authority to exercising it proactively in state court. The lawyer said federal courts within the Second Circuit have now issued 10 rulings in sports prediction-market litigation, all of which have favored the states.

The Block has reached out to Novig for comment.

#4994. Bitcoin short-term holders send 47K BTC to exchanges at a loss

Bitcoin fell to an 11-week low of $72,670 after slipping below short-term holders’ average cost basis of about $75,700, according to CryptoQuant.

Bitcoin (BTC) short-term holders sent more than 47,000 BTC to exchanges on Tuesday as Bitcoin fell to an 11-week low, with the coins worth less than their purchase price.

Data from onchain analytics platform CryptoQuant showed short-term holders sent 47,025 BTC carrying unrealized losses to exchanges in the 24 hours to midnight UTC at the start of Wednesday.

The tally was among the largest 24-hour totals in the four months of data tracked by CryptoQuant and came as Bitcoin dropped to $72,670, its lowest level since Aug. 20.

The short-term holder metric tracks Bitcoin held for up to 155 days. Short-term holders are generally considered more likely to sell when prices fall, even at a loss.

“On November 10, profitable transfers stood near 1,700 BTC, meaning approximately 27 BTC arrived at a loss for every 1 BTC transferred at a profit,” CryptoQuant said.

The largest 24-hour inflows of BTC carrying unrealized losses since late July occurred on Oct. 8, hours after Bitcoin fell back to $80,000. Rolling 24-hour inflows peaked at 50,500 BTC.

Short-term holders’ exchange inflows of BTC carrying unrealized losses. Source: CryptoQuant

Cointelegraph previously reported that short-term holders’ average cost basis was around $75,700.

Related: Stock ETF buyers pounce on dip as Bitcoin funds bleed $1.6 billion

Short-term holders moved into an aggregate unrealized profit on Aug. 20 as Bitcoin rebounded toward $80,000. They slipped back into an aggregate unrealized loss on Friday.

STH cost basis vs. BTC/USD. Source: CryptoQuant

CryptoQuant head of research Julio Moreno said Bitcoin could still be in the downtrend that began in November 2025.

“If rallies keep stalling near the cohort’s cost basis and net unrealized losses deepen, the August rebound starts to look like a pause in the broader downtrend,” he wrote on Monday.

STH-NUPL vs. BTC/USD. Source: CryptoQuant

CryptoQuant’s net unrealized profit/loss (NUPL) metric showed that short-term holders reached their highest level of unrealized profitability since November 2025 as Bitcoin climbed to $87,200 in September.

#4991. Nvidia Erases 2026 Gains as Republicans Threaten Jensen Huang With Subpoena

Nvidia Erases 2026 Gains as Republicans Threaten Jensen Huang With Subpoena

Nvidia shares plunged to $166.95 Tuesday afternoon, leaving the AI chipmaker down 18.42% for November and wiping out all the gains it had accumulated in 2026.

The selloff accelerated as congressional Republicans threatened to subpoena CEO Jensen Huang over his reported relationship with a suspected Chinese intelligence operative.

At that price, Nvidia was roughly 10.5% below its $186.50 closing price at the end of 2025 and 31.4% below its recent $243.37 peak. November is shaping up as its worst month since September 2022, when shares lost roughly 19.6%; the company suffered an even deeper 32% crash in April of that year.

Tuesday's drop exceeded 6% in afternoon trading, after a 4.3% fall on Monday. The two-day decline was on track to erase more than $470 billion in market value. Even after the significant drop, some analysts would still view the stock as extremely overbought in the long term, with prices much higher than what would be considered safe based on technical indicators.

Just for context, even an additional 20% crash (-43% from October) would be considered healthy in an isolated technical analysis, leaving the price still above the average price of the last 50 months. Prices tend to either bounce or trade near that line. When prices go too far away from the average, that usually means there is an unusual appetite for the asset, typically driven more by FOMO than fundamentals.

House Foreign Affairs Committee Chairman Brian Mast told ABC News on Tuesday that he was prepared to subpoena Huang unless the CEO responded by Wednesday, November 11, to a request for a closed-door briefing. No subpoena had been issued at the time of the report.

Mast and two other Republican committee chairs asked Huang to appear on November 18 and explain his interactions with Alice Huang, a former executive at Singapore-based chip buyer Megaspeed. The lawmakers also want details about Nvidia's safeguards against foreign espionage, according to a letter reviewed by Reuters.

U.S. authorities have reportedly investigated whether Alice Huang, who has no family relationship to the Nvidia CEO, passed information to Beijing. The allegations have not established that Jensen Huang knowingly disclosed confidential information or violated export rules.

On Sunday, Nvidia's board announced an independent investigation into whether its chief executive was targeted by a foreign intelligence operation. The board said it had no reason to believe that national security or company secrets were compromised, but would examine the available information.

Earlier today, the Financial Times reported Nvidia has hired former U.S. prosecutor Joon H Kim to conduct the internal investigation.

The company is also facing legal pressure. Shareholders filed a proposed class-action lawsuit Monday alleging that Nvidia and Huang failed to disclose risks associated with the reported relationship.

The scandal has landed during a broader reassessment of the AI boom. Investors have questioned how long massive spending on data centers can continue, and Nvidia's competitors are offering customers more alternatives to its expensive chips.

Google introduced new AI chip configurations Monday. The Information reported that OpenAI was considering buying Google's tensor processing units, or TPUs, which are processors built specifically to run AI workloads. Neither company has confirmed a purchase but yesterday Google announced a new line of TPUs available for select customers, directly competing against Nvidia’s chips.

Nvidia is scheduled to report quarterly earnings on November 17, one day before the congressional briefing requested by House Republicans. Mast's deadline for Huang to respond comes first, on November 11.

#4989. Crosschain protocol Across restores systems after domain hijack

Looking for institutional-grade crypto trading? Discover LMAX Digital, the regulated exchange from LMAX Group

Security platform GoPlus reported that total user losses are estimated at $350,000, with funds stolen from wallets on Ethereum, Arbitrum, Base, BNB Chain, Ink, and Plasma.

Crosschain interoperability protocol Across Across Protocol (ACX) has restored control of its website and resumed normal operations on Wednesday after a domain takeover that redirected visitors to a phishing site.

At around 18:45 UTC on Nov. 10, attackers gained control of the protocol's official domain through a social engineering attack on its domain registrar, Namecheap, Across said in a post on X.

Across said it received an alert within 15 minutes of the takeover and began efforts to take the site offline. Security response group SEAL911 flagged it as a phishing domain with major wallet providers, while Cloudflare marked the domain at risk and subsequently removed its DNS records, rendering the phishing site inaccessible.

While Across did not disclose the number of impacted users or losses, security platform GoPlus reported that total user losses are estimated at $350,000, with funds stolen from wallets on Ethereum, Arbitrum, Base, BNB Chain, Ink, and Plasma.

GoPlus detailed that the phishing page lured users into signing fake token approvals or permits designed to drain wallets.

Later on Wednesday, Across announced that it had regained control of the domain with assistance from Namecheap, and restored all systems to normal operations. It added that all affected users would be made whole.

Separately, decentralized exchange Arcus said on Tuesday that it lost around $2.23 million in USDG stablecoins due to an accounting bug that allowed an account to record a larger balance than it actually held. The exploiter requested another $3 million in withdrawals that were prevented by the rate limit on its bridge.

The DEX said no other user accounts were impacted, and it has fully covered the temporary shortfall with the company treasury.

Suiball, a Sui-native hardware wallet project, also announced closure on Tuesday, after the Sui Foundation decided that backing the project was too risky due to a recent wave of attacks on crypto hardware wallets.

These incidents follow a surge in security breaches and phishing campaigns accelerated by AI tools that enable faster and more sophisticated attacks. DefiLlama data shows $8.78 billion has been lost across 341 crypto hacks and exploits year-to-date, led by a $6.36 billion breach at Binance.

#4988. Trump Media Posts $410 Million Profit. Bitcoin Gains Did the Heavy Lifting

Trump Media Posts $410 Million Profit. Bitcoin Gains Did the Heavy Lifting

Trump Media & Technology Group reported a $409.7 million third-quarter profit on Monday, a sharp turnaround for the company behind President Donald Trump's Truth Social.

However, most of the money that Trump Media & Technology made didn't come from media or technology. It came from the rising value of its crypto holdings and related investments.

The company generated just $2.97 million in revenue during the three months ended September 30, according to its SEC filing. A year earlier, it had reported a $54.8 million loss.

Trump Media recorded $496.1 million in realized and unrealized gains on digital assets, pledged crypto and equity securities, according to its earnings release. Much of that was an accounting gain—assets rose in value even when the company hadn't sold them for cash.

Digital assets and pledged coins accounted for $378.1 million of those gains, with securities adding another $118 million. Derivative losses and other expenses offset part of the windfall.

Bitcoin supplied a major boost. Chief Financial Officer Phillip Juhan said on the earnings call that it rose about 42% during the quarter, from roughly $59,000 at the end of June to $84,000 at the end of September.

Trump Media held approximately 15,649 Bitcoin at quarter-end, including around 11,385 coins pledged to a program designed to generate additional returns. Some of those assets are subject to arrangements allowing the counterparty to reuse the coins, according to the filing.

The company expanded into crypto in July 2025, purchasing about $2 billion in Bitcoin and related securities. That decision has tied its earnings closely to changes in digital-asset prices.

The downside was obvious just three months ago. Back in August Trump Media lost $238 million in the second quarter as declining crypto prices battered its holdings. This quarter, the accounting effect went in the opposite direction.

Truth Social's underlying business improved, though from a small base. Revenue increased 205% from about $973,000 a year earlier, supported by subscriptions for Truth+ streaming and Truth API, a paid service that gives customers faster access to posts on the platform that critics have likened to insider trading.

The company also recorded growth in advertising sales and fees from its Truth.Fi funds. Even so, Trump Media reported a $12.3 million adjusted EBITDA loss, a measure that excludes investment gains and certain other items, compared with a $29.1 million loss a year earlier.

The latest quarterly profit has not repaired the longer-term picture for shareholders. DJT shares traded around $4.20 Tuesday, up about 3.3% on the day but down 94.7% from their $79.38 registered back in 2024. The stock has lost nearly 70% this year.

Trump Media still posted a $234.2 million net loss for the first nine months of 2026. It is also pursuing a $6 billion merger with fusion-energy developer TAE Technologies, which shareholders are scheduled to vote on December 14.

Before that vote, holders of the company's $1 billion in convertible notes can demand cash repayment on November 30, according to its quarterly filing.

#4985. Bitcoin and Blockchain Basics

Looking for institutional-grade crypto trading? Discover LMAX Digital, the regulated exchange from LMAX Group

How the First Cryptocurrency Works in Practice

Bitcoin is a decentralized digital currency that operates on a peer-to-peer network and is secured through Proof of Work. Its blockchain is a public ledger made up of blocks of transactions, with each block referencing the previous block’s hash to create a verifiable, tamper-evident history. Private keys allow users to authorize the spending of their Bitcoin. Wallet software manages these keys, constructs and signs transactions, and broadcasts them to the network, where nodes independently verify them.

Bitcoin and blockchain basics are the starting point for understanding the cryptocurrency industry. This Campus 101 subject explores where Bitcoin came from, how a blockchain records transactions, why decentralization matters, how mining secures the network, and how users control and send Bitcoin.

The lesson summaries below give a short overview of the full course content. Take the full interactive Bitcoin & Blockchain Basics 101 course to explore each lesson in more depth through guided explanations, examples, and activities.

Establishes Bitcoin and blockchain as the starting point for understanding mining, monetary policy, wallets, transactions, and the wider crypto industry.

Connects Bitcoin’s creation to the 2008 financial crisis, Satoshi Nakamoto’s whitepaper, decentralized money, and early contributors.

Defines Bitcoin as peer-to-peer digital money with a fixed supply, Proof of Work security, and transparent but pseudonymous transactions.

Uses a digital-notebook analogy to explain blocks, hashes, hash pointers, Merkle trees, tamper evidence, and verifiable ledgers.

Explains how miners perform Proof of Work, find valid hashes, add blocks, and earn block rewards and transaction fees.

Covers the 21 million supply cap, predictable issuance, block rewards, halvings, and Bitcoin’s scarcity model.

Introduces market capitalization, hash rate, and mining difficulty as different measures of market size and network activity.

Distinguishes backward-compatible soft forks from incompatible hard forks, using SegWit and Bitcoin Cash as examples.

Lesson 8 - Securing Communication and Information

Explains how private keys, public keys, and digital signatures establish control and authorize transactions.

Compares hardware and software wallets and explains addresses, private-key protection, and seed-phrase recovery.

Introduces UTXOs, inputs, outputs, change, fees, mempools, and the path from signing to confirmation.

Considers Bitcoin as peer-to-peer money, a store-of-value asset, a commodity-like asset, and the foundation of a wider decentralized economy.

Bitcoin launched during a turbulent economic period, when many people were losing trust in traditional financial markets. Cryptocurrency offered an alternative approach: a financial system designed to operate without central authorities, banks, or intermediaries.

Satoshi Nakamoto’s 2008 whitepaper, Bitcoin: A Peer-to-Peer Electronic Cash System, outlined the idea of a decentralized, trustless, censorship-resistant form of money. Satoshi’s identity remains unknown, but the network launched in 2009 and became the starting point for the broader cryptocurrency ecosystem.

Other important topics include early contributors and thought leaders, including educators, cryptographers, and early adopters who helped make Bitcoin understandable, secure, and culturally important.

Bitcoin is presented as the first cryptocurrency. It is a decentralized digital currency with a fixed supply of 21 million coins, a peer-to-peer network, Proof of Work security, and transparent but pseudonymous transactions.

The important beginner idea is not only that Bitcoin is digital money. It is that Bitcoin showed how a network could record value transfers without relying on a single company, government, or bank to update the ledger.

Decentralization means the distribution of power and control across a network, rather than relying on a single entity. In Bitcoin, thousands of independently operated nodes verify and store transaction data. This helps ensure that no single party can easily alter, censor, or control the network.

Instead of trusting one central database, the network relies on many participants to maintain and check the ledger.

A useful mental model is this: imagine a blockchain as a digital notebook. Each page of the notebook is a block. Each block contains information such as transaction data. Each block is assigned a unique code, called a hash, that identifies its contents.

Each block also points back to the previous block. That link is what creates the chain. If someone tries to change the contents of an already verified block, the hash changes, making tampering apparent to the rest of the network.

Within each block, Merkle trees combine transaction hashes into a compact commitment to the block’s contents. Rewriting confirmed history would require an attacker to redo the Proof of Work for the altered block and catch up with the honest network, which makes tampering increasingly difficult as more blocks are added.

Mining is introduced as the work that secures the ledger. In Proof of Work, network participants compete to solve computational puzzles. The first miner to find a valid solution earns the right to add a new block of transactions to the blockchain and receive compensation through newly issued Bitcoin and transaction fees.

Miners act as accountants competing to validate Bitcoin transactions. Mining rigs perform many calculations to find a nonce that creates a hash matching the difficulty set by the network. Other nodes then check whether the proposed block follows the rules before accepting it.

Bitcoin’s supply is capped at 21 million coins. New Bitcoin enters the network through mining rewards, and those rewards are reduced through events known as halvings. This creates a predictable supply schedule because no centralized party can simply change the issuance schedule on its own.

Several common metrics describe different parts of the system. Market capitalization combines price and circulating supply to estimate the market value of issued Bitcoin. Hash rate estimates the computational power contributing to mining, while mining difficulty adjusts the challenge of finding a valid block so issuance remains relatively consistent as hash rate changes.

The focus then moves from the network to the user. Bitcoin uses asymmetric cryptography, including private keys, public keys, and digital signatures, to prove ownership and authorize transactions without revealing the private key itself.

A private key is like a secret PIN, password, or physical key. Whoever controls the private key controls the wallet’s assets. A public key is derived from the private key and acts more like the lock or mailbox: it can be shared, but it does not reveal the private key.

A Bitcoin address is a public identifier derived from the public key. It is shorter and easier to share, and it is where someone can receive Bitcoin.

Software wallets run on connected devices and prioritize convenience, while hardware wallets keep signing keys in a dedicated device and reduce some forms of online exposure. Many wallets use a seed phrase as a recovery backup. Anyone who obtains the private key or seed phrase may be able to control the funds, so neither should be shared.

Bitcoin uses the UTXO model, which stands for Unspent Transaction Output. A paper-bill analogy makes it easier to understand: you must spend the whole output and receive change back if needed.

If Alice has one UTXO worth 2 BTC and wants to send 1 BTC to Bob, she spends the full 2 BTC output as the transaction input. The transaction creates one output to Bob and another output back to Alice as change. The original output is then marked as spent and cannot be used again.

Transaction fees help miners decide which transactions to include in blocks. If the network is busy and a fee is too low, the transaction may remain in the mempool, a temporary holding area for valid transactions that have not yet been included in a block.

Other important topics include the difference between soft forks and hard forks. A soft fork is a backward-compatible upgrade, meaning updated nodes can still interact with older nodes. A hard fork is not backward-compatible and can lead to separate chains if participants do not agree on the same rules.

The final point is that Bitcoin now plays several roles in the financial and technological landscape. It is often discussed as a store-of-value asset, a highly liquid digital asset, and the foundation that introduced a broader decentralized economy.

Bitcoin: The first cryptocurrency and a peer-to-peer digital monetary network.

Blockchain: A ledger made of linked blocks that record transaction data.

Node: A network participant that stores, checks, or relays Bitcoin data.

Proof of Work: A consensus mechanism where miners expend computational resources to add valid blocks.

Miner: A participant that competes to create new blocks and earn rewards and fees.

Hash: A unique code generated from data; changing the data changes the hash.

Private key: The secret credential that authorizes control of funds.

Public key: A public value derived from a private key and used in transaction verification.

Bitcoin address: A shareable identifier used to receive Bitcoin.

UTXO: An unspent transaction output that can be used as an input in a future transaction.

Mempool: A temporary area for valid transactions waiting to be included in a block.

After Bitcoin and Blockchain Basics, useful next topics include:

Bitcoin is a decentralized digital currency operated by a peer-to-peer network and secured by Proof of Work.

A blockchain is a ledger made of blocks that point back to previous blocks, creating a tamper-evident chain of transaction history.

Decentralization distributes control across many participants instead of relying on one central operator.

Miners compete to find a valid hash, add a new block, and receive rewards and transaction fees.

A wallet helps a user manage private keys, public addresses, and transaction signing.

A UTXO is an unspent output from a prior transaction that can be used as an input in a future Bitcoin transaction.

#4982. Bitcoin Miner HIVE Eyes $45 Million in Annual Revenue From AI Deal

Bitcoin Miner HIVE Eyes $45 Million in Annual Revenue From AI Deal

Bitcoin miner HIVE Digital Technologies is eyeing $45 million in annual revenue from an AI data center in Sweden, but it still hasn't secured a final lease.

In its latest earnings update, HIVE reiterated that its 32-megawatt Big Boden facility could generate the sum through a long-term colocation arrangement. That revenue depends on a definitive agreement, financing, facility upgrades, and the project entering service.

The company first disclosed the proposal in June. It involves an unnamed, investment-grade Swedish technology company and contemplates a lease lasting up to 10 years. During Tuesday's earnings call, CEO Aydin Kilic said the parties were negotiating deposits, termination rights, and other terms that could affect financing costs. A lender's consultant had visited the site the previous week, he said.

“We do not want to rush into a deal just for the sake of reporting new top-line revenue,” Kilic said, according to the earnings call transcript.

HIVE expects to seek financing for the Boden project after securing a signed lease, potentially through corporate bonds. The company has not announced a completion date for negotiations.

The existing data center would also need a substantial makeover. HIVE plans to retrofit it for up to 10,000 Nvidia GB300 GPUs, using liquid cooling to handle high-density computing racks.

Although the facility has a 32-megawatt utility connection, the proposed customer would receive approximately 25 megawatts of usable computing capacity, with the remaining power supporting data center operations.

“This site has evolved from a GPU compute facility supporting Ethereum,” Kilic said when announcing the letter of intent, describing its planned conversion into a more powerful AI installation.

The expansion follows HIVE's broader effort to move beyond Bitcoin mining, a shift that has already produced several large GPU-cloud contracts.

HIVE signed a major three-year AI computing agreement with Bell Canada and Cohere in June, covering 2,304 Nvidia GPUs as it expanded beyond crypto mining.

HIVE now reports $185 million in contracted GPU-cloud annualized run-rate revenue, although only $40 million is live. The remainder represents contracted capacity that has not yet entered service.

The company is targeting approximately $700 million in total high-performance computing annualized revenue by the end of 2028, though that figure includes projects requiring additional financing, construction, and customer commitments.

For Boden, HIVE's next milestone is securing a definitive lease before proceeding with project financing.

Deutsche Bank and Deutsche Asset Management signed a memorandum of understanding covering tokenized money market funds, crypto-asset exchange-traded commodities (ETCs), and stablecoins. The firms will look at custody and settlement arrangements, as well as liquidity management, treasury, and distribution, using Deutsche Bank's DAC platform. Manfred Bauer, chief product officer at Deutsche Asset Management, said the firm was planning to launch a tokenized money market fund.

Deutsche Asset Management already offers crypto-asset ETCs and is involved in AllUnity, the stablecoin venture backed by DWS, Flow Traders, and Galaxy. In May, The Block reported that AllUnity planned to introduce a Swedish krona-backed stablecoin, SEKAU, alongside its existing euro- and Swiss franc-denominated tokens.

Deutsche Asset Management said it had €1.22 trillion ($1.36 trillion) in assets under management as of Sept. 30.
